Preferred: the host collector starts the guest collector through wsl.exe and reads its samples from stdout as one more provider, so the host collector stays the only writer of raw files. Do not specify SQLite raw files written through /mnt/c in WAL mode.
- **Cost.** Two sentences
- **Decision.** Guest collector transport: (a) host collector runs the guest collector via wsl.exe and reads stdout, no network and no new file writer; (b) guest writes closed, non-WAL files under /mnt/c that the host imports after close; (c) loopback API under mirrored networking (Windows 11 22H2+, .wslconfig change, a write route, the token in the guest). Recommendation: (a).
- **Verification.** Re-read the cited lines and re-fetched the Microsoft networking page; every quoted sentence is present. Changed the proposed fix: the lead's 'raw files through /mnt/c' would put a SQLite WAL database across the VM boundary, and SQLite says (re-fetched https://www.sqlite.org/wal.html) 'All processes using a database must be on the same host computer; WAL does not work over a network filesystem.' That sub-point is suspected (not run under WSL). Option (a) rests on Microsoft's filesystems page: 'Run Linux binaries from the Windows Command Prompt (CMD) or PowerShell using wsl <command>' and piping works (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/wsl/filesystems).
- **Audit.** Overlap: B8 proposed reserving an ingest route instead; this finding's no-route design supersedes it. H1 may cut phase 7.
- **Leads.** D-privacy#12. Earlier findings touched: R05


### R105. The lock's pid file outlives a crashed holder, so a pid-based daemon stop could signal an unrelated process

- **Lens** D, fixed rule 1 · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** privacy_recheck.out.txt P13 (child takes InstanceLock and exits via os._exit, skipping release): 'holder() returns a pid: True; that pid is alive now: False' / 'lock can be taken again: True'. files.py:229-230 writes only os.getpid(); holder() (files.py:233-238) returns it unchecked; only release() deletes it (files.py:243-244). docs/17:199 `daemon start|stop|status|install|uninstall` does not say how stop finds its target.
- **Impact.** Pids are reused. A stop built as 'signal holder()' after a crash can end an unrelated process of the user, breaking observe-only (fixed rule 1).
- **Proposed change.** src/submeter/store/files.py: write 'pid create_time' to the pid file; holder() returns None unless a non-blocking lock probe fails (lock really held) and the live process's create time matches. docs/17 Operations: '`daemon stop` asks the program to exit (an API call, a stop file or the service manager) and never signals a pid it has not verified.'
- **Cost.** About 10 lines plus a test, one sentence
- **Verification.** Reproduced with my own child process in a temporary folder. The risk is latent: a stop through the service manager (scheduled task, systemd user unit) signals no pid, and docs/14:281 calls the pid a convenience, but holder() is the only handle the baseline offers. Medium kept.
- **Audit.** Overlap: Its holder() fix also covers W8's holder() point.
- **Leads.** D-privacy#15. Earlier findings touched: R30


### R106. Workload names are not validated: reserved names can be claimed, and markup or paths pass through

- **Lens** D, attribution · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** privacy_recheck.out.txt P14: Rule accepted workload 'system', 'submeter', '', '<img src=x onerror=1>', '../../x'; a user rule {workload='system', exe=miner} gives 'aggregate rows: [('system', 1), ('unattributed', 1)]'. rules.py:56 'workload: str' unconstrained; reserved names at rules.py:34-37 (SYSTEM is declared but the attributor never assigns it); aggregate merges by name (rules.py:205). docs/17:98 reconciliation has a 'system' term and docs/17:182 ties overhead_fraction to workload 'submeter'. docs/brief.html:5418, 5423, 5428, 5508 insert w.name with innerHTML.
- **Impact.** A natural rule such as workload = "system" for svchost or systemd merges user processes into the System row and the reconciliation's system term. `run --as submeter` would inflate the overhead_fraction alert. Unchecked names reach run file names, group names and, if the mock is copied, dashboard HTML.
- **Proposed change.** src/submeter/core: one WorkloadName type, ^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._:-]{0,63}$, refusing reserved names (system, restricted, unattributed, submeter, machine) unless the attributor assigns them; AlertRule workload patterns use the same set plus '*'. Use it for Rule.workload and `submeter run --as`. docs/17 Dashboard: 'Insert text from data with textContent, never innerHTML' (this also covers process and executable names, which any local process chooses). Add a test.
- **Cost.** About 10 lines plus a test, one sentence
- **Verification.** Reproduced the accepted names and the merge with my own probe; confirmed the four innerHTML lines in brief.html. Checked the proposed pattern against every example name in docs/08 and examples/rules.toml ('agent:*', 'opaque:wsl', 'browser', 'ide', 'agent:untagged', 'builds'): all fit, with '*' needed only for alert patterns. R29 limits how many names exist, not which ones.
- **Leads.** D-privacy#16. Earlier findings touched: R29


### R107. Recorded fixtures from the work computer would be pushed to a public repository; only command lines must be redacted

- **Lens** G, D · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** yes
- **Evidence.** AGENTS.md:41 covers command lines only ('... or written into a test fixture'). ProcessCounters carries exe, cmdline and cwd (models.py:76-78). docs/13-build-list.md:46 and docs/17:256 ask for 'recorded fixtures for every provider and detector'. prompts/01-execution-prompt.md:3 'on my Windows 11 work computer'. CLAUDE.md:10 'Push freely ... No push here waits for my approval.' The task brief states the repository is public.
- **Impact.** A recorded snapshot of a managed work computer (security products, internal tools, user and host names, folder paths) becomes public on the first push. Fixed rule 3 is a product rule, but this sends the same machine data off the machine through the build.
- **Proposed change.** AGENTS.md after line 41 (needs the owner's approval), or docs/17:256 if not: 'A recorded fixture is anonymised before it is written: user name, host name and home folder replaced; executable names outside an allow-list replaced with stable placeholders; command lines in exe_only form; working folders dropped. A test fails if any fixture contains the current user or host name.'
- **Cost.** Two sentences and a test of about 20 lines
- **Decision.** Where the fixture-anonymising rule lives: AGENTS.md after line 41 (owner approval required; applies to every session) or docs/17 'Also deliver' (no approval needed but weaker)? Recommendation: AGENTS.md, with the same sentence in docs/17.
- **Verification.** Re-read AGENTS.md:36-46, CLAUDE.md:10-12, prompts/01:3 and docs/13:46; no rule covers exe names, paths or host and user names in fixtures. Did not query the repository's visibility (it would put the repository name into a web request); took it from the task brief, as the lead did. Medium kept: it affects buildability and the owner's data, not a number or a product rule.
- **Leads.** G-buildability#12. Earlier findings touched: R04


### R108. The layer rule forbids the imports the planned collector loop and detectors need, and tests/test_layers.py misses relative imports and cannot express 'no program imports another'

- **Lens** E, F, G · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** yes
- **Evidence.** Re-ran f-consistency/layer_rule.py: 'collector imports store FAILS', 'collector imports attribution FAILS', 'analysis imports store FAILS'. tests/test_layers.py:9-18 puts store, collector, attribution, analysis all at layer 2; :43 requires same package or a strictly lower layer. docs/14-baseline-modules.md:304 store.raw 'Imported by: Nothing yet. The collector loop will, in phase 1'; docs/13-build-list.md:17 puts adaptive cadence and overhead guard in component 07 collector.sampler; docs/17-build-prompt.md:269 'the collector loop ... around the existing sampler'. Re-ran g-buildability/layer_rule_cases.py: 'collector imports the raw store, relative: layer test passes; module exit 0'; 'core imports the store package by name: layer test passes'; 'new package server, not in LAYER: ERROR KeyError'; with server/launcher at layer 3, 'server imports the collector: layer test passes'. tests/test_layers.py:23-24 keeps only node.module, so 'from ..cli import app' is invisible (mutation_check: 'layers: core imports cli through a relative import SURVIVED'). docs/17:46 'Five layers ... providers, sampler, attribution, store, analysis' contradicts AGENTS.md:37.
- **Impact.** The first phase-1 task that wires the loop where docs/13, 14 and 17 place it turns pytest red, and each builder will invent a different home for the loop. Relative imports and program-to-program imports pass silently, so the boundary that keeps the collector replaceable (docs/17:56) has no test.
- **Proposed change.** docs/14 (figure, store.raw and store.query 'Imported by') and docs/17:46: the running loops live in a new layer-3 package beside cli (e.g. `daemon` or `programs.collector/.server/.launcher`); collector, attribution, store and analysis stay libraries. tests/test_layers.py: add the new package(s) to LAYER; resolve ImportFrom.level > 0 against the module's package; expand `from submeter import x`; add a PROGRAMS set and a test that no program package imports another. Propose the matching AGENTS.md:37 wording to the owner.
- **Cost.** About 25 lines in tests/test_layers.py, about ten lines across docs 14 and 17, one proposed line in AGENTS.md
- **Decision.** Where do the collector loop and server wiring live? (a) new layer-3 program package(s) beside cli, (b) raise collector to layer 3, (c) order the layer-2 packages. Recommend (a): the four libraries stay independent and the program boundary becomes testable.
- **Verification.** Re-ran both probes; output matches the saved .out.txt line for line. Re-read tests/test_layers.py:9-43. Tried to refute by finding a doc that places the loop above layer 2: none in docs/13, 14, 17 or prompts/01; AGENTS.md:37 only says 'Add a new package to its LAYER table'. growth.py docstring says detectors choose the tier, so analysis->store is a real planned edge. Nothing is broken today (baseline uses absolute imports only). Merged three leads; E#13's relative-import fix belongs here.
- **Leads.** E-baseline#14, F-consistency#1, G-buildability#9


### R109. The named stack needs runtime dependencies beyond the three AGENTS.md allows, and nothing says dashboard assets are served locally

- **Lens** D, F, G, fixedrule3 · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** yes
- **Evidence.** AGENTS.md:46 'No new dependency without my approval. There are three at runtime: pydantic, psutil and typer'; pyproject.toml:6-10 matches; uv.lock has 0 entries for fastapi, uvicorn, starlette, websockets, httpx, pyarrow, mcp, tzdata, pywin32 (grep). docs/17:38 'FastAPI for the local API, with a WebSocket'; 13:63 and 17:142 Parquet export; 17:39 and docs/11:25 'a canvas chart library such as uPlot'. grep of docs for cdn|vendor|bundle|offline|ship inside: no hit that places the chart library locally. Re-fetched https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/how-to/custom-docs-ui-assets/: 'By default, those files are served from a CDN.' and 'To disable them, set their URLs to None'.
- **Impact.** P4 to P6 tasks stall mid-wave for dependency approval or break AGENTS.md:46. A dashboard that loads uPlot from a CDN, or FastAPI's default /docs page, makes the browser fetch from a third-party host, against fixed rule 3.
- **Proposed change.** docs/13 'Alongside the code' and docs/17:38-39: list the runtime dependencies version 1 expects for owner approval at planning, and add 'All dashboard assets, including the chart library and fonts, ship in the package and are served by the server; the page requests nothing from another host. FastAPI is created with docs_url=None, redoc_url=None.' Record the approvals in docs/review/02-rulings.md and commit pyproject.toml and uv.lock once before wave 1.
- **Cost.** Five lines of docs, one decision list, one pyproject and lock commit
- **Decision.** Approve FastAPI + uvicorn (+ websockets; httpx dev-only) with the docs UI disabled, or a stdlib server with polling? Drop Parquet from version 1? Hand-write the MCP stdio server? Add tzdata for Windows? Recommend: approve the FastAPI bundle, defer Parquet, hand-write MCP, tzdata with a win32 marker, ctypes for Windows calls, vendor uPlot with its licence.
- **Verification.** Re-read AGENTS.md:46, pyproject.toml, docs/17:38-39, 13:63; grepped uv.lock and docs. Re-fetched the FastAPI page and confirmed both quoted sentences. Considered whether docs/17:38 naming FastAPI counts as the owner's approval: docs/README.md:72-75 ranks 17 last and gives no dependency list, so the conflict with AGENTS.md:46 stands. Rule 3 is not broken yet (nothing built), so medium, not critical.
- **Audit.** Absorbed 'FastAPI's default /docs and /redoc pages load scripts from a CDN, and the brief does not forbid external references'. Its evidence: FastAPI (re-fetched https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/how-to/custom-docs-ui-assets/): 'By default, those files are served from a CDN.' and 'The first step is to disable the automatic docs, as by default, those use the default CDN.'; download links point to cdn.jsdelivr.net (swagger-ui-dist@5, redoc@2). docs/17:38 names FastAPI; docs/17:39 'Use a canvas chart library such as uPlot' does not require it to be bundled; docs/04:37 'Self-contained HTML reports' is not defined. uv (re-fetched https://docs.astral.sh/uv/concepts/projects/sync/): 'when uv run is used, the project is locked and synced before invoking the requested command.' docs/17:199 does not say what the scheduled task or unit runs. Its proposed change: docs/17-build-prompt.md 'Stack and conventions': 'Create the FastAPI app with docs_url=None, redoc_url=None and openapi_url=None. Every script, style, font and image the dashboard and reports use ships in the package and is served by the server; no page references another host. The dashboard sends Content-Security-Policy default-src 'self'; reports carry a meta CSP default-src 'none' with hashes for inline code. The scheduled task and the systemd unit run the installed entry point, not uv run.' Add a test that fails on any http(s):// reference in the built front end or a report. Audit reason for merging: The CDN fact holds; the verifier re-fetched FastAPI's 'By default, those files are served from a CDN'. But no server exists (src/submeter has no FastAPI app, and B2 found no fastapi in uv.lock), so this is a gap in the specification. A break needs a builder to keep the defaults and a user to open /docs. The request carries no Submeter data, and fixed rule 3 (AGENTS.md:11) lists upload, webhook, telemetry and update check, so no rule is broken. High needs a job or gate to fail, and none does. B2 already covers the same gap at medium: its proposed change says 'All dashboard assets ... ship in the package ... FastAPI is created with docs_url=None, redoc_url=None', and its verifier judged 'Rule 3 is not broken yet (nothing built), so medium'. Two findings proposing the same sentence at different severities should be one. Correction: Fold P11's additions into B2: openapi_url=None, a CSP of default-src 'self' on the dashboard and a meta CSP on reports, and a test that fails on any http(s):// reference in the built front end or a report. P11's 'uv run' sub-point is a suspected issue about what the scheduled task runs, and it belongs with W7, which specifies the task but not the command.
- **Leads.** F-consistency#14, G-buildability#7; D-privacy#11. Earlier findings touched: R04


### R110. The 20 baseline tests catch 1 of 29 deliberately broken behaviours

- **Lens** E · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `plan` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** Re-ran e-baseline/mutation_check.py (with PYTEST_ADDOPTS=--basetemp in a temporary folder so it could not collide with other pytest runs): output identical to the saved file. 'unchanged copy: 20 passed'; control caught; only 'a jump of the monotonic clock is no longer a gap' caught; '28 of 29 broken behaviours survived'. Survivors include I/O reported as running total, rule priority ignored, a rule beating kernel-group membership, workload memory not summed, CPU pending at exit and at rollover thrown away, no keyframe after the first, WAL off, histograms not time-weighted, every bucket well covered, an I/O-restricted process dropped, idle counted as busy, config layers reversed, a default and a range differing from docs/08, minute retention ignored, every weekly file expired.
- **Impact.** AGENTS.md:45 'A change in behaviour starts with a failing test' has nothing to start from for most promises in docs/14, so parallel builders can break attribution, raw-store and rollup behaviour that sets the numbers with no gate failing.
- **Proposed change.** tests/: a planned task adding focused tests for the survivors (about ten tests: sampler I/O delta, rule priority and kernel-group precedence, aggregate sums, raw exit/rollover/keyframe/WAL, rollup weighting and coverage, provider restricted-I/O and idle, config precedence and docs/08 defaults/ranges, choose_tier and expired_minute). The relative-import part of the layer test is in B1.
- **Cost.** About ten tests as one planned task
- **Verification.** Reproduced the run exactly. Checked the mutants are not equivalent (each changes an observable output for some input). Caveat added: the 29 mutants were chosen to target behaviours the reviewer suspected were untested, so '1 of 29' describes that sample, not a coverage percentage; docs/14:383 is right that exact sums, rollup totals and the histogram bound are tested directly.
- **Leads.** E-baseline#13. Earlier findings touched: R10


### R111. Settings validation accepts unknown alert metrics, misspelt thresholds, unselected bad profiles, negative, zero or NaN retention, and crashes on missing or UTF-16 files

- **Lens** E · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** Re-ran e-baseline/config_cases.py: "metric 'cpu_coress' ... accepted", "'commit_fraction' on workload 'browser': accepted", 'verdict.thresholds.cpu_tyme = 0.85: accepted', 'bad values in a profile, no profile selected: accepted', 'profile = 5, profile selected: CRASH TypeError', 'raw = -5 / nan / disk_budget = -1 / min_slope = -1 / cooldown = -60 / unattributed_alert = "250%": accepted', 'UTF-16 ... CRASH UnicodeDecodeError'. New probe verify-8/settings_holes.py: at 00:00:10 UTC 'raw = -5 ... expired_raw -> [2026-11-20.db]', 'raw = 0 ... expired_raw -> [2026-11-20.db]', 'raw = nan ... expired_raw -> []'; 'config check MISSING: exit 1, exception FileNotFoundError'; 'config show MISSING: exit 0'. Code: config.py:153 metric: str; :143 thresholds: dict[str, float]; units.py:137-140 Duration/Size/Rate/Fraction have no range; cli.py:47 silently ignores a named --file that does not exist; cli.py:125 calls load_settings without catching OSError. Docs: 08:130 'A rule that names any other metric is refused when the settings are loaded'; 14:173 'Unknown keys are errors'; AGENTS.md:42.
- **Impact.** A typo in an alert or threshold silently does nothing; a bad laptop profile passes config check and fails at run time; retention.raw of 0 or less lets expired_raw list yesterday's raw file right after midnight, before the hourly rollup reads it, and NaN means raw files never expire by age.
- **Proposed change.** core/config.py: AlertRule.metric as a Literal of the seven ids in docs/08:134-140 with the applies-to check (machine / submeter); Verdict.thresholds as a model of named fields; validate every profile at load; finite ranges for every Duration, Size, Rate and Fraction, with retention.raw at least 24h (docs/17:141 floor) and unattributed_alert 0 to 100%. docs/08 'Other settings': add a range column. cli.py: a named --file or --rules that is missing, undecodable or invalid exits 2 with one line per problem. Tests first for each.
- **Cost.** About 40 lines of code, a range column in docs/08, two tests
- **Verification.** Re-ran config_cases.py (matches the saved output) and wrote settings_holes.py to test the retention consequence and the CLI path directly. Refinement: the lead's 'non-negative' fix is not enough, since retention.raw = 0 also expires yesterday's file at 00:00:10; a floor is needed. Partly a spec gap: docs/08's 'Other settings' table gives no ranges, which is why nothing refuses them. R18 made alert rules structured but its fix ('validated like every other setting') is not met for metric names.
- **Leads.** E-baseline#17. Earlier findings touched: R18


### R112. core/models.py cannot express most of the docs/06 catalogue, and every component edits the same few shared files

- **Lens** E, G · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `plan` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** e-baseline/interface_cases.out.txt: '10 of 14 catalogue rows have no field in Bucket'; "Availability('opaque'), as docs/03 prints for vm.guest: refused" (docs/03:61). models.py:131 SampleBatch.cpu_busy_s is one float, though SystemCounters.cpu_busy_s (models.py:91) is per core and docs/06 keeps load per core in raw and minute tiers; WorkloadSample (models.py:135-147) has no epoch and no USS/PSS field. g-buildability/shared_files.out.txt: 'Record subclasses in core/models.py: 10', 'setting groups in the one Settings class: 10', 'commands in cli.py today: 5' of 13 groups, 'named packages missing from LAYER: [alerts, launcher, mcp, report, server, web]'. AGENTS.md:38 'Subclass Record in core/models.py'.
- **Impact.** Each later component must change the same frozen records and the raw schema, so parallel builders serialise on core/models.py, core/config.py and cli.py, and each schema change migrates raw files already on disk.
- **Proposed change.** docs/PLAN.md: one restructuring task before wave 1 with one owner: add the catalogue fields as Optional (None = unavailable), per-core busy in SampleBatch, OPAQUE in Availability, an epoch on WorkloadSample and raw SCHEMA_VERSION 2; allow new records in their own core modules (AGENTS.md:38 rewording for the owner); cli.py as a package with a module per command group; new tests in tests/test_<component>.py; all new packages in LAYER.
- **Cost.** About 60 lines of fields plus about 50 moved lines, as one planned task
- **Verification.** Re-read models.py:1-187 and docs/06 metric catalogue. Partial refutation: prompts/01-execution-prompt.md:82 (stage 2 item 4) already requires a file ownership map naming pyproject.toml, uv.lock, core/models.py, core/config.py, cli.py, AGENTS.md and STATUS.md with one owner, so the ownership rule exists; what is left is the missing fields and a restructure so one owner does not serialise every wave. Some catalogue rows (system gauges) may belong in a separate system series rather than Bucket, but no record exists for them either.
- **Leads.** E-baseline#22, G-buildability#10. Earlier findings touched: R27, R32


### R113. The run file's life is contradictory: written only at the end yet needed while live, 'until read' yet needed for re-attach, two candidate writers of final totals, and no deleter

- **Lens** F · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** docs/17:66 the launcher 'writes the group's totals to its run file when the command ends'; 17:68 'The collector finds live groups from the run files ... After a restart it re-attaches to every live group'; 17:67 'a small detached holder, or the collector. Choose one and test it'; R15 (docs/16:147) settled 'held open by a detached helper'; AGENTS.md:43 and docs/05:52 make the launcher the only writer of run files; docs/06:17 runs/*.json 'Kept for: Until read'; docs/05:44 'registers the group with the collector' although the collector has no API; store/files.py:26 Program = Literal['collector', 'server'] and files.py has runs_dir but no expiry for it.
- **Impact.** A run file written only at exit cannot tell a restarted collector which groups are live, and deleting on 'read' can remove it before final totals land, so a run's exact cost (job 1, P2 gate 'its final totals survive its exit') is lost or written twice.
- **Proposed change.** docs/06 runs row, docs/17:66-68 and :107, docs/05:44: 'The launcher writes the run file when the group is created (state live) and replaces it by atomic rename with final totals when the group empties (state final). When the command leaves processes behind, a detached copy of the launcher holds the group and writes the final state; the collector never writes it. The server copies a final run file into history's run table and then deletes it.' Delete 'or the collector' from 17:67.
- **Cost.** About six sentences across 05, 06 and 17
- **Verification.** Re-read every cited line. Refutation attempt: docs/README.md:75 makes the topic files (incl. 16) win over 17, so R15's detached helper already decides the holder question; the 'or the collector' in 17:67 is a defect, not an open decision, and needs_decision drops to false. The live/final timing, the 'until read' lifetime and the missing deleter are not settled anywhere. Overlaps G-buildability#8 items 2-3 (cluster B16), kept here.
- **Leads.** F-consistency#5. Earlier findings touched: R15, R23, R26


### R114. The 13 API routes cover neither cost nor runs, the state-changing commands have no route or writer, and R05's ingest route is missing

- **Lens** F · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** docs/13:71-83 lists health, capabilities, config, epochs, workloads, workloads/{id}/processes, series, verdict, findings, diff, sessions GET/POST, WS live; none returns session_summary or run rows. docs/13:59 'cost cost per workload, session or run'; R26 (16:219) 'cost and diff read the summaries'. docs/05:50 and the 05 figure route the CLI through the API, while docs/17:46 calls the CLI a thin reader 'over store and analysis'. docs/13:64 'config ... reload | rotate-token' and :66 'store ... backup' act on server-owned files (06:16 backups, 06:20 token), yet 13:82 and 17:254 allow no write but session markers. R05 (16:81) 'The ingest API accepts a second collector'; 17:79 'Design the ingest path'; no route.
- **Impact.** If the CLI goes through the API as 05:50 says, `cost`, `report`, `export` and `store status` have nothing to call; `config rotate-token` and `store backup` can work only by the CLI writing server-owned files (a second writer) or through an unstated channel; phase 7 has no ingest path.
- **Proposed change.** docs/05:50 and 17:46: say which commands read store files directly and which call the API. docs/13 routes and 17:254: add GET /cost and GET /runs if the CLI goes through the API; state that session start and stop are both markers on POST /sessions; add local admin routes (POST /config/reload, /token/rotate, /backups) or name the file channel (see B15); reserve an ingest route for phase 7 or mark R05's ingest out of version 1. Update the count in 13:5 and README.
- **Cost.** About ten lines in 05, 13 and 17
- **Verification.** Re-read all lines. Partly refuted: 'session markers' (13:30) is plural, so POST /sessions can carry stop as well as start; and MCP lives inside the server (17:53), so the P6 'own cost' gate does not need an HTTP route. The root defect is 05:50 vs 17:46 on whether the CLI reads through the API, plus the write commands with no writer and R05's missing route (R05's fix is not reflected in the route list, so it is not rejected as already covered). Overlaps B15 on reload and rotate-token.
- **Audit.** Overlap: Its ingest-route item is decided under P12, which proposes the opposite fix (no route); H1 may cut phase 7 entirely.
- **Leads.** F-consistency#6. Earlier findings touched: R05, R26


### R115. Behaviours named once and never specified: profile selection, quiet_hours format, dashboard token delivery, unended sessions, group membership, kernel totals versus polled sums, the System bucket

- **Lens** F · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** Profile: docs/08:117 'Each machine can carry a named profile' is the only spec; config.py:280 profile= is the only selector, cli.py:111 offers --profile on config show only, config.py:267 skips SUBMETER_PROFILE (config_cases: 'SUBMETER_PROFILE=laptop in the environment: accepted (1 keys set)', the file's key only). quiet_hours: docs/08:98 only; config.py:173 str | None, unvalidated. Token: docs/17:148 token on every HTTP and WS request; no doc says how the browser page obtains it (grep 'token'). Sessions: docs/04:23 and 06:74 'summarised when they end'; grep for unended or overlapping sessions finds nothing. Membership: rules.py:133 takes a `tagged` map; no doc says how the collector learns it. Kernel vs polled: rules.py:213-216 labels a kernel workload KERNEL while :204-211 sum polled processes (attribution_cases section 3: 'source=kernel, cpu_s=0.4: the label says kernel, the figure is the polled sum'). System: docs/02:141 'Kernel time, interrupts, deferred calls'; rules.py:36 SYSTEM is never produced (doc_vs_code section 5: "a 'system' row is produced: False").
- **Impact.** Each is a guess for a builder. The last three decide whether job 1's tagged figures are the kernel's and whether the 2% reconciliation check can fail at all.
- **Proposed change.** One sentence each: docs/08 'How configuration behaves' names the profile selector (SUBMETER_PROFILE, else none); docs/08 Alerts gives quiet_hours as "HH:MM-HH:MM" local time, validated; docs/06 or 17 says how the dashboard gets the token (e.g. a `submeter open` one-time code exchanged for an HttpOnly cookie); docs/04 or 06: a session with no stop is closed at its last covered sample and summarised then, sessions may overlap; docs/05: membership from /proc/PID/cgroup and the job's process list, and a tagged workload's totals are the group's kernel deltas, polled processes feeding only the tree view; docs/02 and 17: define System from counters and report the remainder separately.
- **Cost.** About eight sentences across 02, 04, 05, 06, 08 and 17
- **Verification.** Grepped docs/ (excluding 16) for profile, quiet, token, session end/overlap, membership and System; each appears only at the cited lines. Re-read rules.py:125-233. Corrected a citation: the System text is docs/02:141, not 02:173 (the file has 158 lines). The System derivation likely also sits in a measurement-lens cluster; kept here only as the spec gap.
- **Audit.** Overlap: Three of its items are stated with their evidence elsewhere and are dropped here: the System bucket (R3), 'kernel' totals versus polled sums (R10) and dashboard token delivery (P8).
- **Leads.** F-consistency#11. Earlier findings touched: R07, R15, R26


### R116. docs/17 adds requirements the topic files lack and drops others, so the compact spec and the topic files describe different builds

- **Lens** F · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** grep of docs 01-16 finds no hit for: '150 MB' (17:230), 'synchronous' (17:138), 'idempotent' or 'final 30 seconds' (17:123), 'breakaway' or 'kill-on-close' (17:69), every data file restricted (17:146; 06:20 restricts only the token), 'previous version' (17:140), 'working director(y/ies)' withheld from MCP (17:148; 08:125 says command lines only), 'workload level' growth (17:154). 17:96 'parent chain' vs 08:217 and 14:261 'parent, the program that started the process' and rules.py _direct(process, groups, parent) using the immediate parent. 17:148 'check Origin on the WebSocket' vs 15:25 P4 gate 'requests ... with a foreign Origin are refused' and 13:30. Dropped from 17: known limits 17:218-226 omit docs/10's VRAM (10:13) and Restricted (10:16) rows; 17:274 P6 gate omits 'own cost' (15:33); 17:275 phase 7 has no gate (15:37); 17:212 omits the settings view (13:31); 17:94 order omits Submeter itself (14:259).
- **Impact.** docs/README.md:75 says the topic file wins where they differ, and prompts/README says 17 can start a single-session build alone: a builder from 17 checks Origin only on the WebSocket and fails the P4 gate, or builds parent-chain matching the code lacks; a plan from the topic files loses the memory budget, breakaway rule and file permissions.
- **Proposed change.** Write each 17-only item into its topic file or delete it from 17: docs/06 for synchronous=NORMAL, bucket finality, idempotence, file permissions and schema versions; docs/05 or 13 for breakaway and kill-on-close; docs/08 'Fixed on purpose' for what MCP withholds; docs/08 rules paragraph keeps 'immediate parent' to match code and 17:96 follows; docs/07 for workload-level growth; docs/15 for the 150 MB budget. docs/17:148: 'check Host and Origin on every HTTP and WebSocket request'. Copy the missing limits and gates into 17.
- **Cost.** About twenty lines across 05, 06, 07, 08, 13, 15 and 17
- **Verification.** Ran my own greps over docs 01-16 for each 17-only item (no hits except 06:20 and 16:243 for the token), read docs/10 rows against 17:218-226, and read 17:94, 212, 274-275. R04's fix (16:75) says the API 'checks Host and Origin headers' without limiting it to the WebSocket, so 17:148 is the narrow statement. R36 covers durability in principle but does not place synchronous=NORMAL or version acceptance in a topic file.
- **Audit.** Overlap: Two of its items are stated elsewhere and are dropped here: what MCP withholds (P10) and the 150 MB memory budget (M13).
- **Leads.** F-consistency#12. Earlier findings touched: R04, R36


### R117. Several gates need data, hardware or the owner's machine that nothing provides, and some lack a reference or pass criterion

- **Lens** G · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `plan` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** docs/15:9 P0 '`doctor` is correct on both' has no reference; 'Per-process VRAM is checked against the adapter total' needs a GPU; 'over a working day' (15:45) is the owner's day. 17:269 'I will run a 24-hour soak myself' has no pass criterion. 15:21 P3 slow leak needs a multi-day hourly fixture (17:155 'at least 7 days'); '`why` answers in under 2 seconds' names no data volume or machine. 15:25 P4 '30-day range loads in under 1 second' needs 30 days of minute and hourly files that nothing generates. 15:37 P7 needs WSL2: re-fetched https://ubuntu.com/wsl/docs/latest/howto/run-workflows-azure/: 'GitHub runners, however, run as a service.' 'This means that they are not on a user session, hence they cannot run WSL (or any other store application).'
- **Impact.** Builders and checkers would each invent the reference data and procedure, so a gate pass would mean different things in different sessions, and owner-run gates have no written steps.
- **Proposed change.** docs/PLAN.md gates section (prompts/01 stage 2 item 6) must, per gate: name the reference (P0 doctor: a committed expected matrix per runner; on the owner's machine the owner confirms against probe output), the data (synthetic 3-hour raw fixture for the hourly 0.1% check; synthetic 7-day and 30-day stores for P3 and P4), the machine (VRAM, WSL share and P7 are owner procedures), and numeric criteria for the 24-hour soak (overhead, collector memory growth, integrity_check ok, gaps only where recorded). docs/15 and 17:268-274 then restate those references in one line each.
- **Cost.** About 30 lines in PLAN.md, a few lines in docs 15 and 17
- **Verification.** Re-read 15:9-37 and 17:266-275; re-fetched the Ubuntu WSL page and confirmed the quote. Downgraded high to medium and fix now to plan: prompts/01-execution-prompt.md:84 already requires, per gate, 'the exact commands or procedure, the pass criteria as numbers, the evidence to keep, who checks it ... and which gates need me or a second operating system', so writing procedures is stage-2 work by design and no gate fails at run time. Refuted two sub-claims: (1) the hourly 0.1% check can be done in a 30-minute soak that straddles an hour boundary, since the bucket is final 30 s after it ends (17:123), or from a synthetic fixture; (2) the P0 VRAM check needs a probe and a GPU, not component 23.
- **Audit.** Overlap: Naming the synthetic multi-day stores is B17's; this finding keeps the gate-reference items.
- **Leads.** G-buildability#2. Earlier findings touched: R10, R16, R19


### R118. Hosted CI runners are elevated, GPU-less and sessionless, so they cannot stand in for the owner's unprivileged machine

- **Lens** G · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** Re-fetched https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/runners/github-hosted-runners: 'Windows virtual machines are configured to run as administrators with User Account Control (UAC) disabled.'; 'The Linux and macOS virtual machines both run using passwordless sudo.'; standard public-repo rows Linux and Windows '4 CPU, 16 GB RAM, 14 GB SSD'; GPU only under larger runners. Ubuntu WSL page: 'GitHub runners, however, run as a service ... cannot run WSL'. docs/17:266 'Set up CI on Windows and Linux runners in phase 0, because you can only run one OS yourself'; docs/15:9 P0 '`doctor` is correct on both'; 15:13 P1 '30-minute CI soak stays inside the budget' while 17:230 defines the budget 'With 300 processes running'. No .github folder exists.
- **Impact.** On the Windows runner every process is readable, so Restricted handling, OpenProcess failures, the token-file ACL and any accidental need for elevation (fixed rule 2) pass in CI; doctor there reports an administrator's matrix; the P1 soak runs at an unknown process count; logon task, notifications, WSL2 and GPU cannot run there.
- **Proposed change.** docs/15 (P0) and docs/17:266: 'CI proves portability: ruff, mypy (with --platform for the other OS), pytest, fixture parsing and logic on both OSes. It does not prove privilege, hardware or session behaviour, because Windows runners are administrators with UAC off. Tests that need an unprivileged user, a user session (logon task, notifications, WSL) or hardware (GPU, battery, suspend) are marked and run on the owner's machine at the gate. The CI soak states its process count.' Run CI on every pushed branch from wave 1.
- **Cost.** A paragraph in docs 15 and 17; the CI workflow is a P0 task
- **Verification.** Re-fetched both vendor pages; all quoted sentences are present. Downgraded high to medium: prompts/01:3 puts the build session on the owner's Windows 11 work computer, so unprivileged Windows behaviour can be tested locally, and the elevated runner is a usable source for the 'with elevation' column docs/17:268 asks for. The remaining risk is that gate wording names CI as the proof (15:9, 15:13). The claim that the Ubuntu runner lacks a user systemd session for cgroup delegation (component 13/14 Linux half) was not verified here; would be settled by a CI job running `systemctl --user is-system-running; systemd-run --user --scope -p Delegate=yes true`.
- **Leads.** G-buildability#3. Earlier findings touched: R10


### R119. Phase gates depend on components of later phases

- **Lens** G · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** yes
- **Evidence.** P1 gate 'totals reconcile within 2%' (15:13) needs 'the four fixed buckets, reconciliation check', component 15 in P2 (13:25); baseline aggregate() produces no system row (f-consistency/doc_vs_code section 5: "a 'system' row is produced: False"). P1 asks for 'the minute and history files ..., retention and backups' (17:269), but no component is the server process: server.api is component 20 in P4 (13:30) and docs/14:84 lists 'the server' as not built. P2 'A session's cost reads the same before and after its raw data is deleted' (15:17) needs session markers, whose only route is POST /sessions (13:82, P4) while only the server writes history (AGENTS.md:43). P0 'The cost of one full sample is measured' (15:9) with only psutil built (bulk providers are components 04/05 in P1); docs/11:19 'One full sample of 300 processes takes more than 10 ms in phase 0' is the Rust trigger, and the psutil provider measured 111.2 ms median at 447 processes on this Linux machine (probes/measure/snapshot_cost.out.txt). P7 'moves to directly after phase 2' (15:37) but R05's ingest route is in no list (13:70-83).
- **Impact.** Builders either pull later components forward untracked or fail the gate; the Rust decision would be taken on the fallback provider the brief already expects to miss the target.
- **Proposed change.** docs/15 and docs/13: (a) P0 times a minimal bulk-snapshot prototype (or the measurement moves to the P1 gate) and says which provider is timed; (b) move the reconciliation part of component 15 to P1; (c) add a 'server' program component in P1 (rollups, retention, backups, integrity check, run-file ingestion, epochs); (d) say whether CLI commands read store files directly, and give sessions a P2 path; (e) P7 names its ingest route and moves with it.
- **Cost.** About 15 lines across docs 13 and 15
- **Decision.** Do CLI commands read store files directly (API serves dashboard and MCP only), or go through the API, which pulls a minimal API into P2? Recommend direct reads: reading breaks no one-writer rule and keeps P2 and P3 independent of P4.
- **Verification.** Re-read 13:11-35, 15:9-37, 17:268-275, docs/11:17-21 and docs/14:80-84; confirmed the system-row result and the snapshot figure in saved outputs (not re-timed). Downgraded high to medium: no gate fails at run time; the dependency graph prompts/01:83 (stage 2 item 5) asks for would expose these, and the phase order is open to challenge (prompts/01:19). The ingest-route part overlaps B8.
- **Leads.** G-buildability#4. Earlier findings touched: R05, R07


### R120. No stated channel carries stop, a session's interval override or session start to the collector, and Windows has no signal for a graceful stop

- **Lens** G · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** yes
- **Evidence.** docs/05:5 'Three programs share nothing but files on disk'; 17:56 'None of the three imports another'; the collector has no API. Needing a channel: `daemon stop` (13:55); 'A session may override the sampling interval for its own duration' (17:190; 08:74 `session start bench --interval 250ms`) while sessions are written by the server (POST /sessions, 13:82; history.db, 06:15); the launcher 'registers the group with the collector' (05:44) while the run file is written 'when the command ends' (17:66). Re-fetched https://docs.python.org/3/library/os.html (os.kill): 'Windows: The signal.CTRL_C_EVENT and signal.CTRL_BREAK_EVENT signals are special signals which can only be sent to console processes which share a common console window, e.g., some subprocesses. Any other value for sig will cause the process to be unconditionally killed by the TerminateProcess API, and the exit code will be set to sig.'
- **Impact.** On Windows, `daemon stop` can only kill the collector and server (bounded by the 5-second loss rule, 17:138, but never a clean shutdown); a session's interval override and session boundaries have no path to the collector; parallel builders would each invent a mechanism.
- **Proposed change.** docs/05 and docs/06: name files as the only channel and list them, e.g. under state/control/: `stop-<program>` (removed by the program on exit), `sessions/<id>.json` written by whoever starts or stops the session and read by the collector each tick, `runs/<id>.json` written live at launch (see B7); config reload by settings-file mtime. Collector reports (overhead guard, provider status) go as `event` rows in its raw file, which the server turns into findings or epochs.
- **Cost.** A table of about 5 rows in docs 06 and a paragraph in docs 05
- **Decision.** Commands reach the programs through files polled each tick, or through a loopback control endpoint on the collector (a second port and token)? Recommend files: no new listener, same on both OSes, survive restarts, keep the collector replaceable.
- **Verification.** Re-read every cited line (daemon stop is 13:55, not 13:58) and re-fetched the os.kill paragraph. Downgraded high to medium and narrowed: files are already the stated channel (05:5); 'Hot reload ... on file change' (17:187) gives config reload a path through the settings file; and 17:130 'The collector records such events in the raw file and the server turns them into epochs' with the raw `event` table (13:39, raw.py:35) already carries collector-to-server events, so the overhead-guard finding has a path. What is left unspecified is stop on Windows, the session-to-collector path and run-file registration.
- **Leads.** G-buildability#5. Earlier findings touched: R15, R32


### R121. Builders would have to guess the shared interfaces: minute and history table columns, the WS /live messages, the --json shapes and the MCP transport

- **Lens** G · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `plan` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** Columns exist only for the 8 raw tables (store/raw.py:34-53). The minute file (meta, rollup_1m) and history file (meta, workload, epoch, rollup_1h, exe_1h, run, session, session_summary, finding, baseline; docs/13:40-41) have no columns anywhere (grep for CREATE TABLE and the table names: only 05:75-77, 13:40-41, 17:251-252). 'WS /live' (13:83) has no message format, rate or handshake. 'every command accepts --json with a stable schema' (17:197) has no schema; baseline shapes differ: doctor an object with 'version' (cli.py:68), top a bare list (cli.py:96), store status {'bytes','total'} (cli.py:138); config show/check have no --json. 17:53 puts MCP inside the server; 15:33 'its own cost' does not say how a request identifies its run. 17:187 'Settings that need a restart must say so' with no list.
- **Impact.** Parallel builders of the server writers, detectors, API and CLI would define the shared contract differently and meet only at integration.
- **Proposed change.** Planned first P1 task with one owner: the DDL of the 12 minute and history tables in docs/06 (rollup tables mapping to Bucket). Doc paragraphs: a short WS /live message schema and token handshake in docs/13; a JSON envelope {"schema": "submeter.<command>/1", ...} in docs/03 with a snapshot test per command; MCP transport choice and how 'own' run is identified (e.g. SUBMETER_RUN set by the launcher) in docs/17:53; the restart-only settings in docs/08.
- **Cost.** DDL as a planned task; a paragraph each in docs 03, 08, 13, 17; under 50 lines for the JSON envelope
- **Verification.** Re-read raw.py:30-53, cli.py:55-142, docs/13:37-83; grepped docs and src for schema text. Changed disposition from fix now to plan because the main item (12 tables of DDL) exceeds a small doc change. The run-file items (lead's 2 and 3) are kept in B7. The MCP spec quotes were not re-fetched (medium finding); the transport point rests on 17:53 alone.
- **Leads.** G-buildability#8. Earlier findings touched: R15, R26, R04


### R122. A session longer than raw retention cannot be summarised exactly at its end. The brief never says totals accumulate while the session runs. Raw left at the end holds 75% (48 h) or 42% (24 h floor) of a 72 h session, and its top executables cannot be rebuilt from exe_1h

- **Lens** C, F · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** docs/06-storage.md:74 'A session or tagged run is summarised when it ends, so its cost stays exact after its raw data is gone.' docs/04-features.md:23 says the same. docs/17-build-prompt.md:127 'When a session stops or a tagged run ends, store its exact totals per workload: CPU seconds, peak and mean memory, I/O, processes started and top executables.' No document says which tier a summary is built from, that totals accumulate during the session, or how long a session may be. Retention and budget: docs/08:80 retention.raw 48h; docs/17:141 the budget deletes raw 'never going below 24 hours'; docs/08:44 'raw retention about 38 h' and :54 'about 13 h'; docs/08:74 a session may override the interval to 250ms. files.py:121-131 expires whole day files. docs/06:75 says exe_1h keeps the top ten executables per workload per hour. docs/15:17 P2 gate: 'A session's cost reads the same before and after its raw data is deleted'. The R26 fix (docs/16:215-219) assumes the session ends before its raw data expires. No summary code exists in src/ (grep 'session|summar' finds only a cli.py docstring). Probe session_retention.out.txt, 72 h session at a steady 2.0 cores: 'retention 48h (default): expired_raw at session end -> ['2026-11-16.db'] ... raw still on disk covers 2026-11-17 00:00:00 to 2026-11-19 06:20:30: 54.3 of 72.0 h'; 'retention 24h (budget floor) ... 30.3 of 72.0 h'; 'truth: 518,400 CPU-s'; 'raw at end, retention 48h (default) 391,260 CPU-s (75.5% of truth)'; 'raw at end, retention 24h (budget floor) 218,460 CPU-s (42.1% of truth)'; 'minute buckets 518,460 CPU-s if the edge bucket is kept, 518,340 if dropped'; 'hourly buckets (minute tier cut, R147) 520,860 ... kept, 513,660 if dropped (start edge 1230 s into its bucket)'. Constructed top-executable case: 'true session top: steady with 28,800 CPU-s'; ''steady' appears in 0 of 72 hourly top-ten lists'; 'top from the exe_1h lists: burst01 with 21,600 CPU-s'.
- **Impact.** This breaks job 1 for any session longer than raw retention. That is 48-72 h at the defaults, 24-48 h while the disk budget trims raw, and less with a 250 ms session override. The brief's wording invites a summariser that reads raw at the end. Such a summariser stores 75% or 42% of the session's CPU and I/O as 'exact' for good, and cost and diff read only that row. Sums, process starts, and peak and mean memory can be rebuilt from minute buckets to within the start-edge minute, but nothing tells the builder to do so. If R147 cuts the minute tier, the edge grows to as much as an hour (-0.9% to +0.5% here). Per-session top executables cannot be rebuilt from hourly top-ten lists at all: an executable outside one hour's top ten loses that hour. An unended session closed days later (R119) hits the same problem. The P2 gate cannot catch it, because its session ends before any raw data is deleted.
- **Proposed change.** docs/06-storage.md:74 and docs/17-build-prompt.md:127: 'A session's and a tagged run's totals accumulate while it runs. As each raw tick is written, or each minute bucket becomes final, the server updates an open session_summary row in history.db: it adds each workload's CPU, I/O and process starts, updates peak and time-weighted mean memory, and adds per-executable CPU and peak memory. At the end it closes the row. Nothing is recomputed from raw at the end, so a session of any length stays exact.' The open row records the last tick it includes, so that after a restart the server catches up from raw that is still on disk (bounded by R93's record of what has been rolled up). For tagged runs, CPU, I/O and process counts still come from the kernel at the end, and mean memory and top executables accumulate the same way. docs/15:17 and docs/17:270, P2 gate: add 'including a session longer than raw retention (injected clock, retention.raw of a few minutes)'.
- **Cost.** Documents: two sentences, plus one gate clause in each of docs/15 and docs/17. Build: an accumulator in the rollup or history task, about 40 lines and 2 tests, scheduled as plan.
- **Verification.** Searched docs/ for how a summary is built (grep 'summar', 'session_summary', 'exact totals'). The only hits are docs/04:23, 05:77, 06:74, 16:219 and 17:127, and none gives a source tier, accumulation or a length limit. Ran the baseline's own FileMap.expired_raw on placeholder day files; the CPU figures are arithmetic on the stated design. Refutation attempts: (1) Minute buckets keep exact sums, so CPU and I/O can be rebuilt to within the edge minute. That holds, so the verdict is 'with changes' and the severity medium rather than critical. (2) Sessions longer than 48 h may be rare. But the 24 h budget floor, a 250 ms override and unended sessions all shorten the threshold, and no document limits session length. (3) The top-ten case is constructed. Still, any executable outside an hour's top ten loses that hour, so the lists cannot give exact session totals in general. R26, R93, R117, R119, R147 and the H-scope nothing_found line 'Sessions: kept' were all checked, and none states the long-session case.
- **Audit.** Added by the coverage check of the lead reviewer's own leads in 00-working-notes.md.
- **Leads.** working-notes lead 38; coverage probe docs/review/probes/coverage/session_retention.py (parts 1-4). Earlier findings touched: R26, R93, R119, R147


## Low (17)


### R123. A pid reused while psutil's process_iter cache holds the old process is missing from two snapshots, then credited no CPU

- **Lens** A · **Severity** low · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `plan` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** psutil/__init__.py:625-650 is_running() marks the pid reused and raises NoSuchProcess. :666 ppid() calls _raise_if_pid_reused() on every OS. :1540-1550 process_iter() removes the reused pid after computing new_pids, so the next pass skips it as well. psutil_provider.py:91 calls ppid(); :102-103 turns NoSuchProcess into None. verify-1/pid_reuse.out.txt (reuse imitated as psutil's tests do): 'snapshot 1: pid missing from the snapshot' / 'snapshot 2: pid missing from the snapshot' / 'snapshot 3: present, first_seen True, born 4.9 s before this snapshot, credited 0.00 CPU-s'.
- **Impact.** For the psutil provider, a process that takes a reused pid is in no bucket for two ticks, and its first seconds of CPU are never credited, against 'never dropped' (docs/17:78). This is rare on Linux (pid_max 4194304). It matters mainly where psutil is the required Windows fallback (R17); how fast Windows reuses pids is not verified here.
- **Proposed change.** src/submeter/providers/psutil_provider.py: when a pid that psutil.pids() still lists raises NoSuchProcess, read it again in the same tick through a fresh psutil.Process(pid). Add a test that ages the cached object as verify-1/pid_reuse.py does.
- **Cost.** About 10 lines and one test
- **Verification.** I re-read the psutil 7.2.2 paths and wrote my own probe (a `sleep 6` child; its cached object given an older identity and start time; snapshots 1.1 s apart, so the born-this-interval test sees a real cadence). It reproduced the two missing snapshots and a zero credit. The reuse is imitated, not produced, but the code path is real psutil. I kept severity low and moved the disposition from fix now to plan: the change belongs with the phase-1 provider fallback work (R17), and nothing in planning depends on it.
- **Leads.** A-linux#16. Earlier findings touched: R17


### R124. The design names Python 3.12, whose time.monotonic() on Windows steps in 15.6 ms, too coarse for 250 ms intervals

- **Lens** A · **Severity** low · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** https://docs.python.org/3/whatsnew/3.13.html: 'On Windows, monotonic() now uses the QueryPerformanceCounter() clock for a resolution of 1 microsecond, instead of the GetTickCount64() clock which has a resolution of 15.6 milliseconds.' pyproject.toml:5 requires-python '>=3.12'. docs/11:5 'Recommended: Python 3.12 for all of version 1'. docs/01:26 'Python 3.12 throughout'. docs/17:36 'Python 3.12 or newer'. config.py:44 active_interval down to 250ms. psutil_provider.py:43 time.monotonic(); sampler.py:53 interval from its difference.
- **Impact.** On Windows with 3.12, each interval length carries up to ±15.6 ms of error: ±6% on a 250 ms interval and ±1.6% at the 1 s default. That noise goes into per-tick rates and the cores histogram. Totals are unaffected because the intervals telescope. The design explicitly recommends 3.12, so a builder following docs/11 gets this.
- **Proposed change.** pyproject.toml: requires-python '>=3.13'. docs/17:36, docs/01:26 and docs/11:5: name Python 3.13. docs/reference/windows-priorities.md:28 and :68: update to match.
- **Cost.** About five lines
- **Verification.** I re-fetched the Python 3.13 What's New text and the quote is exact. I re-read pyproject and found that the design documents name 3.12 outright, which makes this more likely than the lead stated; this checkout resolves 3.14.5. Nothing was re-timed. I downgraded severity from medium to low: the error is per-sample noise at short cadences, totals are exact, and Windows per-process CPU times may themselves be tick-granular (not verified here), so 3.13 removes one noise source but perhaps not all.
- **Leads.** A-windows#10. Earlier findings touched: R08


### R125. On Windows the per-core busy sum probably counts interrupt and DPC time twice

- **Lens** A, E · **Severity** low · **Status** suspected · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** psutil release-7.2.2 cpu.c, re-fetched: 'kernel time includes idle time on windows' / 'systemt = kernel - idle;'. InterruptTime and DpcTime are returned as separate fields and neither is subtracted. .venv _pswindows.py:272-277 returns (user, system, idle, interrupt, dpc). psutil_provider.py:115-122 computes busy = sum(fields) - idle = user + (kernel - idle) + interrupt + dpc. Microsoft 'Processor Object', re-fetched: '% DPC Time is a component of % Privileged Time because DPCs are executed in privileged mode.' That sentence is about the performance counter. No Microsoft page found says SYSTEM_PROCESSOR_PERFORMANCE_INFORMATION.KernelTime contains DpcTime and InterruptTime.
- **Would confirm.** On Windows: t0 = psutil.cpu_times(percpu=True); w0 = time.perf_counter(); sleep 10; read t1 and w1. If, per core, Δ(user+system+idle) ≈ w1-w0, the double count is confirmed. If Δ(user+system+idle+interrupt+dpc) ≈ w1-w0, it is refuted.
- **Impact.** If confirmed, machine busy time, cores in use and a counter-based System are overstated by interrupt and DPC time. That is usually a fraction of one core, more during a driver DPC storm. In normal use it stays under the 2% reconciliation tolerance.
- **Proposed change.** docs/reference/windows-priorities.md item 3: add 'over 10 s, per core, Δ(user+system+idle) equals Δwall; if so, interrupt and DPC are inside system'. If the check holds, psutil_provider._system() on Windows subtracts interrupt and dpc from the busy sum (one line), and the bulk provider does the same.
- **Cost.** One checklist line now; one line of code after the check.
- **Verification.** Re-fetched psutil cpu.c and the Microsoft Processor object page, and both quotes are there. Read the provider and the psutil Python code. The Microsoft sentence concerns the privileged-time counter, not the NtQuerySystemInformation field, so the finding stays suspected. Downgraded from medium to low: the evidence does not establish the double count, and its usual size, well under one core, neither breaks the 2% rule nor misleads a verdict. In a DPC storm it would make the storm look larger, not hide it. On Linux the formula is right, because iowait and guest are removed.
- **Leads.** A-windows#9, E-baseline#23. Earlier findings touched: R07


### R126. psutil reads command lines through the PEB with more access than needed, once per process per sample; on managed machines Defender's LSASS ASR rule may log it

- **Lens** A, D · **Severity** low · **Status** suspected · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** psutil _pswindows.py:763-775: cmdline() tries the PEB first ('PEB method detects cmdline changes but requires more privileges') and falls back on a permission error. psutil_provider.py:69 calls cmdline() for every process on every snapshot, and platform_check.py:77-92 runs 41 snapshots. Re-fetched ASR rules reference: the rule is under 'Standard protection rules'. 'Some apps enumerate all running processes and attempt to open them with exhaustive permissions. This ASR rule denies the app's open process actions and records the details to the Security log in Windows Event Viewer. This rule can generate numerous noise.' Also: 'This activity generates considerable ASR rule noise, but doesn't block functionality.' The EDR alerts column for the rule is 'N'. 'If you enabled Local Security Authority (LSA) protection ... This ASR rule isn't required.' windows-priorities.md:89: 'If a probe is flagged, stop and tell me.'
- **Would confirm.** On a Windows machine with the ASR LSASS rule in audit or block mode and LSA protection off, run platform_check, then search the Defender Operational log and the AsrLsassCredentialTheftAudited/Blocked hunting events for python.exe.
- **Impact.** No number, fixed rule or job is affected: per Microsoft the rule logs and does not block functionality, and raises no EDR alert. The risk is audit events naming python.exe on a managed computer, which would trigger the owner's stop rule in P0 and draw IT attention. Reading command lines every sample also adds avoidable per-process opens.
- **Proposed change.** docs/17-build-prompt.md:71: 'Open other processes with PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION only, never PROCESS_VM_READ, and read command lines with NtQueryInformationProcess (ProcessCommandLineInformation) once per new process, not per sample.' windows-priorities.md item 7: 'after platform_check, look in the Defender Operational log for ASR events naming python.exe.'
- **Cost.** Two sentences
- **Verification.** Re-read the psutil source and the provider. Re-fetched the ASR page; the quotes hold, but the same page says the rule 'doesn't block functionality', has no EDR alert, and is not required where LSA protection is on. So I downgraded from medium to low. Still suspected: undocumented whether a standard user's open of lsass, which its DACL refuses, reaches the ASR callback at all. Did not re-check event IDs 1121/1122, so I left them out of the proposal.
- **Leads.** A-windows#12


### R127. Windows private bytes come from psutil's 'private' alias with a silent getattr fallback to working set

- **Lens** E · **Severity** low · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** psutil_provider.py:63: `int(getattr(memory, "private", memory.rss))`. Probe verify-3/w10_private_fallback.out.txt: 'without private: provider kept 111000000 (rss is 111000000, vms is 333000000); warnings: none; fell back to working set silently: True'. pyproject.toml:8 'psutil>=6.0' has no upper bound; uv.lock pins 7.2.2. The PyPI JSON shows latest release 7.2.2, so psutil 8 is unreleased. psutil.io/api (dev docs), 8.0.0 changelog: 'pagefile and private -> vms' and 'All these old names still work but raise DeprecationWarning.' psutil 7.2.2 _pswindows.py:819 has `vms = t[7]  # pagefile`. Re-fetched PROCESS_MEMORY_COUNTERS_EX: 'PrivateUsage: Same as PagefileUsage.'
- **Impact.** Nothing breaks at the next relock: in 8.0 the alias still works and only warns. A later release that removes it would switch Windows memory to working set with no error, bringing back the R06 failure that leak detection was designed to avoid.
- **Proposed change.** src/submeter/providers/psutil_provider.py:63: on Windows read memory.vms (PagefileUsage, which equals PrivateUsage, in psutil 7.x; mapped to PrivateUsage in 8.x), and memory.rss elsewhere, with no getattr fallback.
- **Cost.** Two or three lines
- **Verification.** Wrote and ran a probe that replaces psutil's platform memory_info and shows the silent fallback. Checked PyPI and the psutil dev docs: the lead's 'psutil 8 deprecates' refers to an unreleased version that keeps the alias with a warning, so the risk is further off than stated. Found a simpler fix than version branching: vms is already private bytes on Windows in both 7.x and 8.x (Microsoft: PrivateUsage is 'Same as PagefileUsage').
- **Leads.** E-baseline#25. Earlier findings touched: R06


### R128. P0's WSL/Docker rule does not say what the 10% is a share of, or who measures the working day

- **Lens** B · **Severity** low · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** yes
- **Evidence.** docs/15:9 'The share of CPU time held by a WSL2 or Docker VM is reported'; docs/15:45 'Phase 0 reports the VM's share of CPU time over a working day. Above 10%, the guest collector moves to directly after phase 2 ... | Nothing to change. The rule applies itself'; docs/17:264 'report what share of CPU time a WSL2 or Docker Desktop VM holds over a working day. If it is above 10%, tell me'; docs/17:266 'you can only run one OS yourself'. reconcile_terms.out.txt: 'machine busy (per-core counters) 53.96 CPU-s = 22.12% of capacity', so 10% of busy time is about 2.2% of capacity.
- **Impact.** The phase-7 decision can flip with the denominator: busy time and capacity differ by about 4.5x on this machine. From CI runners the builder cannot measure the owner's working day, or the per-process VRAM check (docs/15:9), so the P0 gate needs the owner to run a script. No displayed number changes; what changes is when agents running in WSL become attributable (job 1).
- **Proposed change.** docs/15:45 and docs/17:264: 'share of the machine's busy CPU time held by the VM process (vmmem, vmmemWSL), over at least 8 hours of normal use, measured by a script the builder supplies and the owner runs; the per-process VRAM check likewise'. Report the share against both busy time and capacity.
- **Cost.** Two sentences in docs/15 and docs/17.
- **Decision.** Should the 10% be measured against busy CPU time or against total capacity? Recommendation: busy time, because the question is how much of the work happens out of view.
- **Verification.** Re-read docs/15:9,45 and docs/17:264,266,268. Searched docs/ for 'working day': only these places. Refutation: docs/17:264 says 'tell me', so the owner sees the figure and decides; 'the rule applies itself' is overstated rather than harmful. Downgraded from medium to low. R10 assigns the 24-hour soak to the owner but not the P0 measurements, so no earlier finding covers this.
- **Leads.** B-measurement#15. Earlier findings touched: R05, R10, R16


### R129. The write-ahead log is not truncated at each checkpoint as docs/17 says, and a long-lived reader grows it without bound

- **Lens** C · **Severity** low · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** raw.py:71 journal_size_limit=67108864; truncation happens only in close() (raw.py:233); docs/17:138 says 'truncate it at each checkpoint'. Re-fetched https://www.sqlite.org/pragma.html: 'in WAL mode, the write-ahead log file is not truncated following a checkpoint. Instead, SQLite reuses the existing file'; journal_size_limit truncates 'to the limit'. https://www.sqlite.org/wal.html: 'if there is always at least one active reader, then no checkpoints will be able to complete and hence the WAL file will grow without bound.' Lead's wal_readers.out.txt: '110.1 MiB after an unfinished cursor was held across 4000 ticks (27 KiB a tick); 64.0 MiB 1500 ticks after the cursor closed'. files.py:152 counts history.db only, not its -wal.
- **Impact.** Normal running leaves about 4 MiB of log per open file, which is harmless. After a long reader, such as a database browser the user left open on today's file, the log stays at up to 64 MiB per open file, and disk_use misses the history file's log. The baseline's reader uses fetchall (raw.py:196-201), and docs/17:138 already requires short reads.
- **Proposed change.** docs/17-build-prompt.md:138: replace 'truncate it at each checkpoint' with 'journal_size_limit bounds the log left after a checkpoint; store status and doctor report each -wal size and raise a finding when it exceeds that'. raw.py:71: set the limit to 4 MiB. files.py:152: count history.db*. Correction to the lead's fix: a timed TRUNCATE checkpoint cannot complete while a reader holds an old snapshot, so it does not bound growth under a long reader.
- **Cost.** One doc sentence and two one-line code changes.
- **Verification.** I re-fetched wal.html and pragma.html; the quotes are present. I did not re-run wal_readers.py (4000+ ticks of I/O plus strace while other verifiers are running). I downgraded from medium to low: the brief already requires short reads and the baseline reader complies, so what remains is a doc/code mismatch and up to 64 MiB per file after an incident. I also corrected the proposed fix.
- **Leads.** C-storage#8. Earlier findings touched: R36


### R130. Retention deletes only the .db, so -wal and -shm side files are left behind and counted forever; an open reader defeats 'frees its space at once' on both systems

- **Lens** C · **Severity** low · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** files.py:124 globs '*.db', while disk_use (files.py:154) counts '*.db*'. verify-6/v6_read_path.out.txt: 'expired_raw returns ['2026-11-10.db']; deleting those leaves ['2026-11-10.db-shm', '2026-11-10.db-wal'], which disk_use counts under raw: 1081344 B after the .db is gone'. The lead's side_files.py re-ran identically: a mode=ro read leaves '-shm 32768 B, -wal 0 B'; a killed writer leaves '-wal 3926392 B'. Re-fetched os_win.c winOpen: 'dwShareMode = FILE_SHARE_READ | FILE_SHARE_WRITE;' with no FILE_SHARE_DELETE. DeleteFileW: 'The DeleteFile function fails if an application attempts to delete a file that has other handles open for normal I/O or as a memory-mapped file (FILE_SHARE_DELETE must have been specified when other handles were opened).' Python os.remove: 'On Windows, attempting to remove a file that is in use causes an exception to be raised; on Unix, the directory entry is removed but the storage allocated to the file is not made available until the original file is no longer in use.'
- **Impact.** Orphaned side files, from a few KiB to a few MiB per day (more after a crash), are never deleted and count against the budget. The P1 gate wording 'Deleting a day of raw data frees its space at once' (docs/15:13) is false on both systems while any reader holds the file.
- **Proposed change.** src/submeter/store/files.py expired_raw and expired_minute: return each file's .db together with its -wal and -shm. docs/17-build-prompt.md Store: 'retention deletes the .db with its -wal and -shm, treats a refused delete as retry-next-pass, and counts space as freed only when the files are gone'. docs/15:13: 'frees its space once no reader holds it'.
- **Cost.** About 6 lines and two doc clauses.
- **Verification.** I re-fetched all three vendor sources; the quotes are present. I re-ran side_files.py and wrote v6_read_path.py part 4. Part of the lead is already handled: docs/17:112 ('Open closed files only for the length of a query, so they can be deleted on Windows') and docs/reference/windows-priorities.md:60 ('retention needs to retry later, which the build must plan for') already cover the Windows blocking and the retry. expired_raw is recomputed from disk on every pass, so a refused delete retries without extra code. What is new is the side files and the gate wording, so I downgraded from medium to low. The Windows behaviour itself was not observed here.
- **Leads.** C-storage#9. Earlier findings touched: R23


### R131. daily_minimums applies one UTC offset to every reading, so local days are wrong across a daylight-saving change; the fix needs no tzdata

- **Lens** C · **Severity** low · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** growth.py:102-110: one 'utc_offset_s' is applied to every reading. docs/06:103 'Days and weeks are worked out in your time zone when you ask ... A day of 23 or 25 hours is compared per hour.' verify-6/v6_local_days.out.txt: 'offsets the OS gives across the window, in hours: [-5.0, -4.0]; fixed offset -5'; 'days whose fixed-offset minimum misses the nightly low: ['2026-11-01']'; 'Theil-Sen slope of daily minimums: fixed offset 9.63 MiB/day, per-reading offset 10.02 MiB/day (true 10.00)'; 'tzdata package installed: False'. Re-fetched https://docs.python.org/3/library/datetime.html: astimezone() 'If called without arguments (or with tz=None) the system local time zone is assumed ... with the zone name and offset obtained from the OS.' The lead's local_days.py re-ran identically (Kolkata: '3 of 72 hourly buckets hold parts of two local days').
- **Impact.** Across each daylight-saving change, the slow-growth check's daily minimums put an hour in the wrong day. In a 28-day synthetic leak the slope came out 4% low and one day lost its low. In half-hour zones, local days cut from hourly buckets are off by 30 minutes at each end. The effect on the trend job is small.
- **Proposed change.** src/submeter/analysis/growth.py daily_minimums: drop the fixed offset and take each reading's local day from datetime.fromtimestamp(t, UTC).astimezone(), which gets the offset from the OS with no new dependency. docs/06-storage.md:103: add 'in zones not a whole number of hours from UTC, local days built from hourly buckets are approximate by up to 30 minutes'. Do not add tzdata (AGENTS.md: no new dependency without approval) unless you want named zones other than the machine's own.
- **Cost.** About 5 lines, a test, and one doc sentence.
- **Verification.** I re-ran the lead's probe and got identical output. I also wrote v6_local_days.py to measure the effect on what the detector actually reads, not just bucket counts. I downgraded from medium to low because the slope moves by a few percent. I refuted the tzdata part: the per-reading OS offset needs no zone database package. That ran on Linux with tzdata absent; for Windows it rests on the Python docs' 'obtained from the OS' and was not run there. R35's intent stands; the baseline function cannot express it.
- **Leads.** C-storage#14. Earlier findings touched: R35, R34


### R132. rollup.py merges hourly buckets from minute buckets; docs/05, R13 and docs/17 say hourly buckets are built from raw data

- **Lens** F · **Severity** low · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** yes
- **Evidence.** rollup.py:9-10 'The same functions build minute buckets from raw samples and hourly buckets from minute buckets.' docs/05-data-flow.md:49 'Minute and hourly buckets are both built from raw data.'; docs/05:52 'Hourly buckets are built from raw data'; R13 (docs/16) 'Hourly rollups are computed from raw samples while those still exist'; docs/17:123 'Build hourly buckets from raw data while it still exists.' docs/14:316 'An hour merged from 60 minute buckets equals the hour built directly from samples' (tests/test_baseline.py:423-430).
- **Impact.** R27's mergeable histograms made R13's requirement unnecessary, but the brief still ties hourly roll-ups to raw files, which may already be gone after server downtime (S2). A builder following docs/17:123 reads every raw file twice.
- **Proposed change.** docs/05-data-flow.md:49,52 and docs/17-build-prompt.md:123: 'Hourly buckets are merged from minute buckets.' Note in R13 that R27 superseded it.
- **Cost.** Three sentences.
- **Decision.** The brief and the code disagree (AGENTS.md: say so and ask). Should hourly buckets be merged from minute buckets, as rollup.py does and R27 makes exact, or built from raw, as docs/05, docs/17 and R13 say? Recommendation: merge from minute buckets, and update the brief.
- **Verification.** I re-read every cited line and the test. Both routes work with the baseline today (roll_up with width 3600 also builds from raw), so nothing fails and the impact is low. Neither route is more exact: merge inherits S10's latest-epoch problem, and a direct roll_up takes one logical_cores per call.
- **Leads.** F-consistency#15. Earlier findings touched: R13, R27


### R133. redact_patterns has no validator and no way into the store

- **Lens** D, E · **Severity** low · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** privacy_recheck.out.txt P4: 'Settings accepts the pattern '(': ('(',)' / 'redact_cmdline with '(' raises PatternError: missing ), unterminated subpattern at position 0' / 'RawFile parameters: ['path', 'cpu_points', 'memory_step', 'redaction']' / 'RawStore parameters: [..., 'redaction']'. config.py:185-187 has no validator, while Rule.cmdline is validated (rules.py:69-73). grep: redact_patterns is used nowhere else in src. redact.py:66 compiles extra patterns on every call, and they run per argument (redact.py:81).

- **Verification.** Re-ran the checks in my own probe: confirmed. Downgraded medium to low: 'never applied' is unbuilt wiring (docs/17:241 build order item 6, privacy.redact), not a defect in a running path. What remains verified is the missing validator, which is inconsistent with Rule.cmdline validation, and an interface that cannot take the setting.

### R134. The AGENTS.md checks do not type-check Windows-only code on Linux, and ruff format --check also checks Markdown

- **Lens** E · **Severity** low · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
