### R139. choose_tier takes raw coverage from retention.raw, not from the files on disk. While the disk budget trims raw toward its 24 h floor, a short window 24-48 h back is sent to a raw file that has already been deleted

- **Lens** C, E · **Severity** low · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** no
- **Evidence.** query.py:40-41: 'if age <= raw_retention_s: tiers, reason = [Tier.RAW, Tier.MINUTE, Tier.HOUR], "window is inside raw retention"'. Nothing checks the store. docs/06:72 'answered from the finest tier that covers its whole window'; docs/14:332 'whose retention still covers the start of the window'. docs/17:141 'delete the oldest raw file first, never going below 24 hours'; docs/08:44 'raw retention about 38 h'. files.py:121-131 expires whole day files. Probe part 5: '20-minute window starting 2026-11-17 20:00:00 (40 h back), retention.raw 48h'; 'choose_tier -> tier=raw step=1s'; 'reason: 'window is inside raw retention''; 'files the budget may delete at its 24 h floor: ['2026-11-17.db']'; 'the window lies in 2026-11-17.db; deleted under the floor: True'.
- **Impact.** While the budget is trimming raw, a chart, `why` or process drill-down asks for a window of up to about 33 minutes (2000 points at the assumed 1 s) that starts 24-48 h back. It gets no rows, labelled raw at 1 s, although the minute file still holds that window. Longer windows go to the minute tier through the point budget and are not affected. R100's proposed fix still reads the settings, so it leaves this case open. The same applies to minute buckets that were not rolled up while the server was down (R93).
- **Proposed change.** src/submeter/store/query.py choose_tier: take where each tier's coverage starts from the store, not from the retention settings. That is the oldest raw day file left and its first tick, and the oldest minute file, or a 'covered from' value per tier that retention, the budget and roll-up keep current. Pass it in, and fold this into R100's fix. docs/14:332: 'whose data on disk still covers the start of the window'. Test: with the day file that holds the window removed, a 20-minute window 40 h back is answered from the minute tier.
- **Cost.** About 10 lines and 1 test, done together with R100.
- **Verification.** Read query.py:26-54 and files.py:121-131, and ran the baseline's choose_tier and expired_raw in the coverage probe. Refutation: at default settings, expiry by whole day keeps raw for 48-72 h, so choose_tier's 48 h test is conservative and nothing fails. The defect needs the budget rule (docs/17:141) or the shrunken retention in docs/08, hence low. The budget enforcement is not built, but docs/17:141 specifies it. An earlier draft of the probe put the window 30 h back, which lies in a file the floor keeps; the probe was corrected to 40 h before its output was saved.
- **Audit.** Added by the coverage check of the lead reviewer's own leads in 00-working-notes.md.
- **Leads.** working-notes lead 29 (the part R100 does not cover); coverage probe docs/review/probes/coverage/session_retention.py part 5. Earlier findings touched: R100, R93, R33


## Scope (lens H, 14)


### R140. Take phase 7 (WSL2 guest collector) out of v1 and drop the 'moves after phase 2' rule; v1 shows the VM as one opaque workload and measures its share from its own data

- **Lens** H · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `defer` · **Needs your decision** yes
- **Evidence.** docs/13-build-list.md:11-35 no ingest component; :82 'POST /sessions the only write'; docs/15:35-37 P7 is a gate only; docs/15:45 decision rule; docs/16:81 R05 'The ingest API accepts a second collector'; docs/17:79; docs/10:11; docs/03:61 doctor 'WSL2 VM holds 3% of CPU time'. learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/wsl/networking (re-fetched): 'By default, WSL uses a NAT ... based architecture'; Linux to Windows 'you need to use the IP address of your host machine'; but Windows to Linux 'you can access it from a Windows app ... using localhost', and mirrored mode (Windows 11 22H2+, opt-in) lets you 'Connect to Windows servers from within Linux using the localhost address 127.0.0.1'. sqlite.org/wal.html (re-fetched): 'All processes using a database must be on the same host computer; WAL does not work over a network filesystem.'
- **Impact.** P7 has no component, route, schema or transport, and its conditional move depends on a working-day measurement with no denominator (B-measurement#15). Deferring loses the per-process view inside WSL, which v1 shows as one opaque workload anyway.
- **Proposed change.** As the lead: delete P7 (docs/15:35-37); docs/15:45 'Not in version 1. The VM is an opaque workload; doctor reports its share of busy CPU time from v1 data. A guest collector is designed after v1 if that share is large.' Delete docs/17:79 ingest sentence, :264 sentence and the WSL item in :268; mark R05's ingest part not in v1; add 'a collector inside WSL2 or Docker Desktop' to docs/13 'Not in version 1'; drop 'in phase 7' at docs/10:11; docs/01:29 '8 phases'. State the reason as 'no transport is designed', not 'none can obey rule 3', and record the candidate transports for later: host pulls over WSL localhost forwarding, or guest-written JSON files with atomic rename.
- **Cost.** About 10 lines across docs/15, 17, 13, 10, 16, 01, plus the same passages in docs/brief.html. No code.
- **Decision.** Defer the WSL2 guest collector after v1 and let v1's opaque-VM workload measure the share (recommended), or keep P7 and its conditional move, which first needs a designed transport (host pull over localhost forwarding, mirrored mode, or a non-SQLite file drop)?
- **Verification.** Re-read every cited line; all match. Re-fetched the three Microsoft pages and sqlite.org/wal.html; quotes are present. Correction: the lead's 'P7 has no design that obeys fixed rule 3' is overstated. Microsoft's networking page shows a Windows program reaching a WSL service via localhost under the default NAT mode (host-pull keeps the host API on loopback), and mirrored mode gives guest-to-host 127.0.0.1. The WAL rule rules out only a live SQLite file shared across /mnt/c, not JSON files written with atomic rename like the run files. The 'We recommend against working across operating systems with your files' quote is a performance recommendation ('unless you have a specific reason'), not a correctness rule. The case for deferral rests on no component, no transport and an undefined decision rule, and that holds. Another option, not tested: run the Linux build standalone inside WSL (the compare-versions table lists systemd support for WSL 2), with its own store and no transport. Severity stays medium.
- **Leads.** H-scope#1. Earlier findings touched: R05


### R141. Build tagged runs first (launcher, holder, run file, `run`, `cost --run`, load generator) as phase 1, before the poller and store

- **Lens** H · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** yes
- **Evidence.** docs/reference/windows-priorities.md:83 'This decides whether tagged workloads can be exact, which is the centre of the design' (unverified on Windows). docs/13-build-list.md:23-24 launcher and providers.group in P2. docs/17:66 'A tagged run is therefore accounted for even if the collector was not running'; docs/05:52; docs/06:17 runs/*.json written by the Launcher. src/submeter/attribution/rules.py:196-216: aggregate() sums polled per-process values; kernel_workloads only sets the label. docs/review/probes/a-linux/cgroup_totals.out.txt: 'cpu.stat usage_usec of the group: 10.677 s (-0.01%)'.
- **Impact.** If Job Object facts (nesting, totals after exit, name lifetime) surface in P2, P1's models, raw schema and run-file contract reopen. The owner's main question waits behind P1's soak although it needs no collector.
- **Proposed change.** docs/15 and docs/17:268-270: new P1 'Tagged runs' = component 13 with its detached holder, the final-totals part of 14, the run file, `submeter run`, `submeter cost --run` over run files, the load generator; gate = 500 sub-second children within 2% of the load generator's own accounting, final totals survive exit, `run` returns with the exit code while left-behind processes are still counted. Old P1 becomes P2; live group reading and re-attach move with attribution. docs/13:23-24 phase cells. Settle the run file's life (docs/06:17 'Until read', F-consistency#5) in the same edit.
- **Cost.** Two phase paragraphs in docs/15 and docs/17, two cells in docs/13, plus brief.html copies. No code.
- **Decision.** Reorder so that tagged runs are phase 1 and the poller and store phase 2 (recommended), or keep the brief's order and rely on the P0 Job Object probe (H8) to retire the risk first?
- **Verification.** Re-read every cited line and the probe output (not re-run: it spawns CPU load, and other verifiers are timing). I found no dependency of the launcher on the collector or store: it writes its own run file and baseline core, config and redact code already exist. Downgraded from high to medium: reordering makes no number wrong and fails no gate. The risk is rework later, and H8's owner-run Job Object probe in P0 retires most of that risk before P1 whatever the order. Caveats: `cost --run` over run files differs from docs/17:127 (cost reads stored summaries in history), so it is an interim reader; the new gate's reference must be independent of the kernel figure (G-buildability#1), as the lead proposes.
- **Leads.** H-scope#2. Earlier findings touched: R01, R15


### R142. Cut hot and cold sets: read every process every tick, slow only the costly fields, and make 2 s the default active interval

- **Lens** H · **Severity** high · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `fix now` · **Needs your decision** yes
- **Evidence.** Re-ran docs/review/probes/b-measurement/hot_cold_sampler.py (output identical): A 'credited 30.0 CPU-s of 94.8 (32%), processes 'started' 1620, 'exited' 1620 (truth: 0 and 0)'; B 'cores p50 0.49, p95 11.62 (truth 1.58 both), capacity 8'. Re-ran model_tables.py (identical): 'whole tick + raw store 36.0 ms ... 1s 3.60% (over) 5s 0.72% (ok)'; '10% of 300 processes hot: ... 1s 0.68%'. docs/review/probes/verify-9/scope_evidence.out.txt: direct /proc 2s 1.80%; psutil fallback 95.1 ms gives 2s 4.75%, 5s 1.90% (over). reconcile_terms.out.txt:3 'the smallest use that can be seen in one second is 1% of a core'; getconf CLK_TCK = 100. docs/17:71 bulk call returns counters 'for every process'. docs/08:61,63,65; config.py:84,86; sampler.py:104 hot_keys.
- **Impact.** As specified, hot and cold sets either credit about a third of CPU time and invent 1,620 starts and exits a minute, or show more cores than the machine has. The only in-budget figure at 1 s comes from the variant that is wrong.
- **Proposed change.** As the lead: docs/17:71 'Read cumulative counters for every process every tick; read USS or PSS, command line and working folder on the slow cadence.' docs/17:170-174 and docs/08:61,63,65: active_interval 2s; delete cold_interval and hot_cpu_threshold. docs/04:8, docs/05:12,45 (not :84), docs/13:17, docs/17:238, R02 fix text: drop hot and cold sets. config.py: remove the two fields and their types (lines 49, 67, 84, 86); sampler.py: remove hot_keys; tests/test_baseline.py:130,138,223 follow. docs/08 explorer and the brief.html calculator: drop the cold-interval input. State that 2 s is in budget only with a direct reader; on the psutil fallback the guard lengthens the interval.
- **Cost.** About 12 document lines, about 20 code lines and about 6 test lines removed, plus the brief.html text and its calculator script.
- **Decision.** Cut hot and cold sets and move the default active interval from 1 s to 2 s (recommended; 1.80% of a core per 300 processes with a direct /proc reader on Linux), or keep 1 s and schedule the per-process-interval sampler redesign (B-measurement#3 option 2)?
- **Verification.** I re-ran both probes; their output was identical. I recomputed the arithmetic in my own probe. Corrections: (1) a 0.5% threshold is unresolvable only on Linux (USER_HZ 100); Windows CPU times are in 100 ns units. (2) The 452 processes are this review machine's count (ground/platform-check.txt:8), not the owner's. (3) The 2 s budget holds only for a direct reader: the psutil fallback reaches 4.75% at 2 s and needs at least 3.2 s active and 9.5 s idle, and Windows is unmeasured. (4) docs/05:84 does not exist (the file has 80 lines; the hot set is at :12 and :45). (5) The code cut also touches tests. Going to 2 s lowers polled capture of short-lived children (modelled 29% to 15%; measured 2.9% at about 1.13 s), which widens B-measurement#10's reconciliation gap but does not change its verdict. Severity stays high: the specified feature fails the 2% reconciliation and capacity under real load.
- **Audit.** Overlap: This is the same decision as G3 option (a). Decide them together; H3 adds only the 2 s default active interval.
- **Leads.** H-scope#3. Earlier findings touched: R02

The lead's edit list misses MCP at docs/05:27,50, docs/08:125, docs/17:53,148,193,245,274 and brief.html, so the cost is about 14 lines, not 6. Asking 'what am I costing' mid-run needs live group totals through MCP or through the CLI alike, so there is no MCP-only capability. SUBMETER_RUN is a new contract (from G), not yet in the docs. ### R146. Defer baseline anomaly and slow growth; v1's trend job is leak detection, diff and hourly history; the Windows I/O verdict must stop depending on an undefined baseline

- **Lens** H · **Severity** medium · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `defer` · **Needs your decision** yes
- **Evidence.** docs/08:89-90 slow_growth.window 7d, baseline.learning_period 7d; docs/07:35 days 1-7 raise nothing. docs/07:99 Windows I/O-bound 'Disk queue and latency above baseline'; docs/03:32 'run queue 2.3 per core baseline 0.4'. docs/06:81-82 baseline inputs are CPU time and growth memory only; docs/06:93 disk latency feeds only 'I/O verdict'. docs/01:18 job 3 acceptance names only the leak. grep -rni spike src: only growth.py:4 docstring. docs/06:15 hourly kept.
- **Impact.** Two detectors needing weeks of real data would be built against fixtures with known defects (B-measurement#8, #9; F-consistency#10). Job 2's Windows I/O-bound verdict cannot fire, because its system-level baseline is defined nowhere.

### R151. Build one OS's platform layer in v1; the other OS runs polled with portable CI

- **Lens** H · **Severity** high · **Status** verified · **Disposition** `plan` · **Needs your decision** yes
- **Evidence.** docs/13:14-15 (04 Windows bulk, 05 Linux procfs), :23-24 (Job Object and cgroup). docs/17:266 'you can only run one OS yourself'. prompts/01-execution-prompt.md:3 'my Windows 11 work computer'. None of that code exists yet.' Lead counts: A-linux 16, A-windows 12.
- **Impact.** Every platform phase carries two kernel-accounting designs and two gates. On Windows the second can be checked only in CI as administrator, so a gate passed there can fail on the owner's machine as a standard user.

The other OS runs polled: `submeter run` reports tagged runs unavailable and runs the command polled, and its CI runs the portable tests. On psutil alone, the second OS runs with the guard at 3.2 s or more active and 9.5 s or more idle (Linux figures), so 'keeps the psutil provider' needs that stated, or the cheap /proc reader (7.5 to 15.5 ms) kept. AGENTS.md:47 (code passes on both) still holds through portable CI. Severity stays high: the second OS's gates cannot be verified under the owner's conditions.
- **Leads.** H-scope#12. Earlier findings touched: R10

## Nothing found


**A-linux**

- Kernel CPU accounting for a tagged group is exact here: 200 sub-second children, cpu.stat 10.677 s against a wait4 sum of 10.678 s (-0.01%); a Delegate scope's payload 0.6465 s against 0.6469 s.
- A mkdir group's totals (cpu.stat, memory.peak, pids.peak) stay readable after its last process exits; the directory remains until rmdir.
- A background process left by the command stays in the group (its parent becomes the user's systemd), and the group cannot be removed while populated (EBUSY).
- Nested groups are hierarchical: an inner group's CPU counts in its parent and stays there after the inner group is removed.
- systemd-run --user --scope passes the command's exit code through (7).
- ProcessKey is identical between two Python processes (448 of 448, bit for bit) and against /proc field 22 + btime in both float orders, as long as the clock is not stepped.
- These docs/09 Linux rows hold as stated: CPU from /proc/PID/stat (readable for every process); PSS/USS from smaps_rollup own-user only; /proc/PID/io own-user only; hwmon temperatures (4 chips); cpufreq plus thermal_throttle counters (8 of 8 CPUs, Intel); battery; RAPL root-only (mode 0o400, owner uid 0); connections own-user only (system-wide socket list readable, 32 of 90 with no visible process).
- PSI cpu, memory and io are readable system-wide and per cgroup. The system 'full' line for CPU is present but zero, and the design's Linux CPU verdict uses 'some', which is correct.
- The Linux bottleneck verdict's memory signal (PSI memory full, docs/07:98) is sound; the commit problem is limited to the commit_fraction alert and tile.
- No probe cgroup is left on the machine (find /sys/fs/cgroup -name 'submeter-probe*' gives 0); every probe printed 'removed: True'.

**A-windows**

- Job creation without elevation: AssignProcessToJobObject requires only JOB_OBJECT_ASSIGN_PROCESS on the job and 'PROCESS_SET_QUOTA and PROCESS_TERMINATE' on the process, which a launcher holds on its own child. No privilege is named. Nesting exists since Windows 8, so the Windows 11 target is fine.
- Job accounting after exit: JOBOBJECT_BASIC_ACCOUNTING_INFORMATION TotalUserTime and TotalKernelTime cover 'all active processes associated with the job, as well as all terminated processes no longer associated with the job, in 100-nanosecond ticks', and TotalProcesses counts terminated ones. That matches the brief for CPU and process counts. Memory is the exception, reported above.
- Same-process second handle on the lock: LockFile 'Exclusive locks cannot overlap an existing locked region' plus the second-handle sentence, so windows-priorities item 2.2 holds. Locking one byte past EOF of an empty file is documented: 'You can lock bytes that are beyond the end of the current file.'
- psutil 'restricted' on Windows behaves as the brief says (10-known-limits.md:16): cpu_times, memory_info (private field) and io_counters fall back to the whole-system query rather than raising AccessDenied, so CPU time and working set are counted. Only an unreadable command line or working folder marks a process restricted. name() uses SystemProcessIdInformation (no handle), so the provider's outer AccessDenied branch is rarely reached.
- WSL2 and Docker VM CPU on the host: Hyper-V scheduler types page: 'Starting with Windows 10, version 1803, the root scheduler is used by default on client systems only', with 'the NT scheduler manag[ing] all aspects of assigning work', so guest CPU is charged to a host process and the P0 'VM share' measurement is possible on Windows 11 client.
- Kernel object namespaces: job names default to the per-session namespace, and creating them under Global\\ needs no privilege (the SeCreateGlobalPrivilege check covers only file-mapping and symbolic-link objects).
- SQLite WAL lifecycle on close: sqlite.org/wal.html: 'When the last connection to a database closes, that connection does one last checkpoint and then deletes the WAL and its associated shared-memory file', which matches raw.py close(). Readers of a closed WAL-mode file need write permission on the directory to create -shm and -wal (same user, so fine). 'WAL does not work over a network filesystem': the data folder must stay on a local disk, as windows-priorities item 6 already requires.
- Python 3.13 and 3.14 clocks on Windows: monotonic is QueryPerformanceCounter at 1 microsecond and time() is GetSystemTimePreciseAsFileTime. Only 3.12 is affected (reported above).

**B-measurement**

- docs/05 polling formula and table: (L/T)(1-e^(-T/L)) with L=300 ms gives 6.0%, 28.9% and 67.8% for 5 s, 1 s and 250 ms, matching the 6/29/68% table (model_tables.out.txt section 1)
- docs/08 explorer table: all 11 rows reproduce exactly from its stated formulas, including the status lines and N=96/GB hours (model_tables section 2). The defect is the assumed costs, not the arithmetic
- First-sighting rule (sampler.py:84-85): crediting a child born since the last poll with its whole CPU is consistent with docs/05's 'credited up to its last sighting'; in reconcile_terms, first sightings were 0.48 CPU-s
- Collector memory against the 80 MB rule on Linux: resident 39 MB, unique 35, proportional 36 with 447 processes and the raw store (collector_footprint)
- Observe-only rule: sampler, rollup, growth and the psutil provider only read; no probe or baseline path changes a process's priority, affinity or state
- Measured raw volume: 3.0 M rows and 0.36 GB projected per 48 h, under the 2 GB cap (120 bytes per row against 90 assumed; covered in the cost-model finding)
- Probe folder passes ruff check and ruff format --check (7 files); the deterministic probes reproduced with zero diff on rerun in the previous attempt

**C-storage**

- ISO-week file names: 4383 days from 2024 to 2035 each map to a week file whose Monday-to-Sunday span holds them, the names sort in time order, and 2026 and 2032 get a W53 (retention_calendar.py part 1). 2027-01-03 maps to 2026-W53.db, as it should.
- Whole-day and whole-week expiry: with checks every hour for 70 days, 3 raw files spanning 48 to 71 h and 5 to 6 minute files spanning 30 to 37 days, matching docs/06 '2 to 3 daily files' and '5 to 6 weekly files'.
- SIGKILL during writes: 12 of 12 trials 'integrity ok' and 0 acknowledged ticks missing from workload rows. The one-transaction-per-tick commit holds; only held per-process CPU is lost (reported).
- A read-only reader in a second process while the writer runs: 40 polls, 0 errors, counts rising throughout.
- Closing order: once a read-write connection opens and closes the file, the -wal and -shm are removed. Only read-only last closers leave them.
- Recreating a deleted .db beside a stale -wal: integrity ok, and the stale log was not applied (side_files.py part 2).
- docs/08 results table: all 11 rows recompute exactly from the printed formulas; docs/06 disk table and docs/05 daily volumes recompute too (budget_arithmetic.py parts 1 to 3).
- docs/03 store status sample: the 11 sizes add to 1192 MB, shown as 1.19 GB, and the expiry notes are consistent with 09:00 UTC on 20 November.
- Raw bytes per row: 48 B synthetic and 59 B for real sample rows (process_raw 48 B, workload_raw 80 B), against 90 B assumed, so the raw estimate is conservative. In a 2-minute real file 66% of bytes are per-day tables, and the command text is stored twice, in the table and in its UNIQUE index (raw_tables_real.py).
- Raw table list in raw.py matches docs/13 exactly.
- Rollup merge keeps sums, extremes and histograms exact when buckets are filled one accumulator per window (bucket_membership approach C: +0.00% in all four cases).

**D-privacy**

- Baseline network use: doctor --json, top, config show, config check and store status opened 0 sockets, and src/submeter imports no network or process-starting module (no_network.out.txt)
- uv in a synced environment: `uv run python -c pass` made 0 connect() calls (uv_run_network.out.txt)
- Typer 0.29.2 tracebacks: no local variables by default, since the baseline app does not set pretty_exceptions_show_locals (error_output.out.txt; Typer 0.23.0 changelog)
- Baseline fixed rule 1: no nice, affinity, suspend, kill, terminate, rlimit or ionice call in src/ (the one kill in scripts/platform_check.py:52 ends the script's own child)
- Baseline fixed rule 2: no runas, sudo or ShellExecute anywhere in src/ or scripts/
- Store choke point: the tested form (flag then value, split argv) never reaches disk, whatever the caller does (raw.py:154; store_bytes 'absent flag then value, split')
- Working folders are never stored: there is no cwd column in the raw schema (raw.py:33-54; store_bytes 'absent working folder')
- CLI --json output in the baseline: top prints workload totals and doctor prints folder paths; no command lines
- DNS rebinding: the Host check in docs/17:148 covers it, provided it applies to every route (which F8 asks for)
- The process tree in the mock is set with textContent (brief.html:5471), so a command line shown there cannot inject HTML; only workload names are at risk (F16)

**E-baseline**

- Sampler pid reuse and first sighting: a new start time is a new process, an older first sighting is credited 0, one born in the interval gets all its CPU (sampler_cases case 6).
- psutil process_iter cache and pid reuse: the provider's ppid() call runs psutil's is_running() check (psutil/__init__.py:459-469, 625-650), so a reused pid is dropped for one snapshot, not given the old process's key.
- Linux system busy sum: fields minus guest add to the elapsed time within 0.05 s (provider_cases case 5).
- Attribution cycles and ties: a parent cycle ends in Unattributed, equal CPU keeps first-seen order (attribution_cases case 5). Deep chains fail only past about 1000 levels (RecursionError at 1500 children-first), which real machines do not produce.
- Raw store exact sums through exit, UTC midnight rollover and keyframes: used 1.699, stored 1.699; keyframes at 0 and 600 s from midnight in the new file (raw_store_cases case 4).
- Instance lock on Linux: a second holder is refused, the lock is free after the holder is killed, the pid file goes on clean release (lock_cases).
- mypy strict on src and tests passes both as Linux and with --platform win32 (25 files).
- Python version: ruff with target py312 flags a PEP 649 forward name and PEP 758 unparenthesised except, which Python 3.14 accepts and 3.12 rejects (version_gate), so the unpinned 3.14.5 does not hide those.
- Probe folder passes ruff check and ruff format --check (16 files).

**F-consistency**

- Counts recomputed by counts.py: 25 components, 12 baseline tags, 18 distinct table names (20 when meta is counted per file; docs/13's 18 means distinct names), 13 commands, 13 API routes, 12 glossary terms, 37 findings (14, 8, 15 by pass; 34 fixed, 3 accepted), 8 phases
- docs/14 module facts: 15 modules, 2,356 lines, each per-module line count, 26 files (19 described plus 7 empty __init__.py), 25 files for mypy, 20 tests, pyproject 49 lines; all match
- docs/14 Imports and Imported-by table: 15 rows, 0 differences from the AST (module_table.py); all 15 Provides lists exactly match the top-level public names
- Sums in samples: docs/02 (4.96 cores = 31%, 20.8 GB = 65%, 26+2+2+1+69 = 100), launch scenario 14.66 cores = 92%, throttle 14.56 cores x 0.62 = 9.0, run-queue model 2.08; docs/03 cost (8.03 of 12.80 = 62.7%), store status (1,192 MB = 1.19 GB, expiry times consistent with files.py at 09:00 UTC); docs/06 disk table (1.25 to 1.65 GB, about 1.6); docs/05 volumes (0.7 M = 8 x 86,400; 13 K = 9 x 1,440; process_raw 3.4 M a day)
- docs/08 sample-time explorer: all 11 result rows recomputed by hand from the stated formulas, including the status rule and N = 96 / GB; all agree (b-measurement/model_tables.out.txt agrees)
- docs/05 polling table (6%, 29%, 68%) and docs/10's 'about 70%' undercount agree with the formula
- docs/08 sampling table: all 11 defaults and ranges plus overhead_limit match core/config.py; other-settings defaults match except thresholds, built-in rules and redact_patterns (built-in rules disclosed at docs/14:177)
- examples/submeter.toml and examples/rules.toml are byte-identical to the samples in docs/08 and load without error
- docs/13 raw-file table list matches the store/raw.py schema (8 tables)
- docs/06 file locations (Windows and Linux) and SUBMETER_HOME match store/files.py
- Attribution order in rules.py matches docs/14:259 (kernel group, Submeter itself, rules by priority, inheritance from a valid parent, Unattributed or Restricted); docs/05 and 17 only omit the Submeter step (in the low finding)
- docs/14's sampler description matches sampler.py (first call returns None, 1 s slack for processes born in the interval, 30 s and 2 s gap rules)
- AGENTS.md's layer order matches the LAYER table in tests/test_layers.py
- Package self-claims: README layout (7 packages, 15 modules, 20 tests), execution prompt is 2,742 words (wc -w), windows-priorities 'runs items 2 to 6', the lock test dependency at windows-priorities:41, prompts/README; all true

**G-buildability**

- uv.lock is universal and carries win_amd64 wheels for pydantic-core, mypy and librt (cp312 to cp314) and for psutil and ast-serialize (abi3 or cp314t), so the lock itself does not block `uv sync` on Windows
- Path length: the longest file inside .venv is 90 characters in the Windows layout and the longest tracked path is 41, so with today's dependencies a worktree prefix of up to about 170 characters stays under 260
- Parallel pytest runs on one machine: _pytest/pathlib.py:46 LOCK_TIMEOUT of 3 days and lock files on live temp folders keep concurrent builders from deleting each other's tmp_path folders
- Job Object nesting and accounting of exited processes are documented for Windows 8 and later (Microsoft Job Objects and AssignProcessToJobObject pages), so the design's premise holds on paper; what remains is the start sequence (finding above)
- Python version pinning and ruff formatting Markdown are real but already covered by lens E (e-baseline/tooling_cases.out.txt, version_gate.out.txt); not repeated
- Same-layer import edges failing the layer test are already covered by lenses E and F; not repeated

**H-scope**

- Python as the language: the measured direct /proc read is 7.5 to 15.5 ms per 300 processes and the collector-shaped process holds 39 MB resident (model_tables.out.txt sections 3-4). The tick's extra cost is Python work after the read, which a 2 s active interval absorbs (1.8%). No language change is proposed; Windows is unmeasured.
- Three separate programs (collector, server, launcher): the split is what lets the launcher ship first and the collector be replaced later. No cut proposed.
- Sessions: kept. After raw expires they are the only exact cost for a period that does not start and end on the hour (R26).
- Hourly top-10 executables per workload (exe_1h): kept, because job 3's 'what inside the browser grew' needs it (R28). Its name-stability problem is lead A-linux's.
- Disk budget, backups and one-instance locks: kept. Their defects are storage and baseline findings, not scope.
- Adaptive cadence: kept as two intervals (active and idle), because a full tick at 2 s costs 1.8% against the 1% idle limit. Hot and cold sets are cut instead.

## Not checked

What the lenses could not verify, and how to check it. Everything that needs Windows is here, because this review ran on Linux.


**A-linux**

- A launcher in a login outside user@UID.service (ssh, a text console): whether systemd-run --user --scope can still move it into a delegated scope. Why: No such session exists here, and creating one (ssh to localhost) changes machine state. The kernel's common-ancestor rule refuses a direct move (verified); whether systemd's user manager gets around it through the system manager is unverified. How to check: From 'ssh localhost' as the same user: systemd-run --user --scope -p 'Delegate=memory pids' -- cat /proc/self/cgroup, and check that the path is under user@UID.service/app.slice.
- A Delegate="memory pids" scope with holder and payload sub-groups. Why: Only Delegate=yes was tested with sub-groups (cgroup_scope 2); Delegate=memory pids was tested for controllers and side effects only. How to check: Rerun docs/review/probes/a-linux/cgroup_scope.py with -p 'Delegate=memory pids' in place of Delegate=yes and confirm that 'moving this process into a sub-group' and 'passing memory and pids down' are allowed.
- A real wall-clock step. Why: Stepping the clock needs root; it was simulated by replacing psutil's boot_time and time.time. How to check: On a disposable VM, as root: read /proc/stat btime, run date -s '-1 hour', read btime again, then run process_identity.py parts 3 and 4 without the simulation.
- A holder as child subreaper (PR_SET_CHILD_SUBREAPER) collecting orphans' I/O and CPU. Why: Not probed; the decision in the tagged-I/O finding depends on it. How to check: A probe that calls prctl(PR_SET_CHILD_SUBREAPER, 1) through ctypes, runs a command that double-forks a 32 MB writer, reaps everything and compares its own /proc/self/io increase with 32 MB.
- WSL2 guest without systemd (phase 7 guest collector). Why: No WSL here. Without a user service manager no delegated subtree exists, so tagged runs would be unavailable. How to check: Inside a WSL2 distribution with systemd off: cat /proc/self/cgroup; systemctl --user status; ls -l /sys/fs/cgroup.
- thermal_throttle counters and NVML on other hardware. Why: This machine has an Intel CPU and an i915 GPU only. How to check: On an AMD CPU: ls /sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/thermal_throttle. On an NVIDIA machine: rerun linux_sources.py and check that NVML loads and gives per-process utilisation unprivileged.
- Windows counterparts named in findings (Job Object peak memory semantics, TotalProcesses, per-process I/O not including children, pid reuse rate). Why: Outside the Linux half; no vendor page was fetched for them in this run. How to check: Read learn.microsoft.com JOBOBJECT_BASIC_ACCOUNTING_INFORMATION and JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION (PeakJobMemoryUsed) and quote them; on Windows, run io_children.py's shape with GetProcessIoCounters.
- Whether reading smaps_rollup of a large process delays that process (it takes the target's memory-map lock). Why: Measuring it needs timing, which the rules forbid while other reviewers run. How to check: On a quiet machine, time a page-faulting loop in a 2 GB process while another process reads its smaps_rollup in a loop, and compare with no reader.

**A-windows**

- Pid 0 size: that the System Idle Process's CPU times equal total idle time on a real machine. Why: Needs Windows; the chain is psutil code plus archived Microsoft text How to check: Standard user: compare Δ(psutil.Process(0).cpu_times().user+system) with Σ Δidle from psutil.cpu_times(percpu=True) over 10 s; expect equal
- Job name lost after the last handle closes while members live. Why: Needs Windows How to check: ctypes CreateJobObjectW('Local\\probe'), child created with PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_JOB_LIST sleeping 60 s, CloseHandle(job), then OpenJobObjectW(JOB_OBJECT_QUERY,FALSE,name): expect NULL with error 2, and IsProcessInJob(child,NULL)=TRUE
- Breakaway refusal and UI-limit nesting failures in real tools. Why: Needs Windows and the owner's toolchain How to check: In a job without BREAKAWAY_OK: subprocess.Popen(..., creationflags=CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB) should raise winerror 5. Launch VS Code, Edge and the agent CLI inside the job and confirm their child processes start; check IsProcessInJob for the renderers
- Nesting under the real launchers (Windows Terminal, VS Code terminal, Claude Code shell). Why: Needs Windows How to check: From each: IsProcessInJob(GetCurrentProcess(),NULL); create the command with JOB_LIST into a new job; expect success, and record any error code
- Whether per-core KernelTime includes DPC and interrupt time (double count). Why: Microsoft documents it only for performance counters How to check: Over 10 s, per core Δ(user+system+idle) from psutil.cpu_times(percpu=True) against Δperf_counter: equal confirms the double count
- Cost of the psutil fallback (one whole-system query per process). Why: Timing needs Windows How to check: With about 300 processes: median of 20 PsutilProvider().snapshot() calls, and the same with num_threads removed; compare with the 10 ms rule
- That SystemProcessInformation returns CPU times and create time for other users' and SYSTEM processes without elevation. Why: Microsoft documents those fields only as Reserved; psutil relies on it How to check: ctypes NtQuerySystemInformation(5) as a standard user; count SYSTEM-owned entries with nonzero UserTime+KernelTime (Reserved1 offsets 0x20/0x28/0x30 on x64)
- Standard user can register a logon task with the settings proposed above. Why: Not settled by the pages read How to check: schtasks /create /tn SubmeterProbe /xml task.xml (LogonTrigger UserId=current user, InteractiveToken, DisallowStartIfOnBatteries=false, StopIfGoingOnBatteries=false, ExecutionTimeLimit=PT0S, Priority 5) without elevation; expect SUCCESS
- GPU Engine, GPU Process Memory and Processor Information counters readable by a standard user. Why: Docs say interactive users can view counters; the specific GPU sets are not checked How to check: PdhOpenQuery, PdhAddEnglishCounterW('\\GPU Engine(*)\\Utilization Percentage') and '\\Processor Information(_Total)\\% Processor Performance', two PdhCollectQueryData calls 1 s apart; expect valid data, run once from the logon task
- Lock release time after TerminateProcess, and reading the locked empty file. Why: LockFile says the release time is resource-dependent; read-past-EOF behaviour is undocumented How to check: 50 trials of kill-holder then acquire every 10 ms, recording the latency; Path(lock).read_bytes() while locked, where PermissionError [WinError 33] means the test fails on Windows
- Defender ASR events caused by the provider. Why: Needs a managed machine How to check: After platform_check, Get-WinEvent Defender/Operational IDs 1121 and 1122 plus Security log entries naming python.exe and lsass.exe
- Job CPU time granularity (tick-sampled or exact). Why: No Microsoft page read states it; it matters for 'exact' at sub-second child lifetimes How to check: Spawn 500 children each busy 5 ms in a job; compare job TotalUserTime+TotalKernelTime with the sum of QueryProcessCycleTime converted at nominal frequency; clustering at multiples of about 15.6 ms means tick sampling
- Monotonic clock across suspend on Python 3.13+ (QueryPerformanceCounter). Why: Already windows-priorities item 5; GetTickCount64 and QPC sleep behaviour pages not re-read in this attempt How to check: submeter top --seconds 60 with a one-minute sleep inside; expect one gap and no CPU burst
- Per-process connections for other users' processes without elevation (the brief says own user only). Why: Not checked against GetExtendedTcpTable documentation How to check: GetExtendedTcpTable(TCP_TABLE_OWNER_PID_ALL) as a standard user; count rows owned by SYSTEM pids
- %APPDATA% redirection or roaming, and Microsoft Store Python's private AppData copy. Why: Python 'Using Python on Windows' page and Folder Redirection docs not re-read in this attempt How to check: Read HKCU\\...\\Explorer\\User Shell Folders AppData and Local AppData; GetDriveTypeW on the data folder (expect DRIVE_FIXED); sys.executable must not be under WindowsApps
- msvcrt.locking maps to LockFile. Why: Python msvcrt and Microsoft _locking pages not re-fetched in this attempt; the LockFile semantics above assume this mapping How to check: Read https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cpp/c-runtime-library/reference/locking and https://docs.python.org/3/library/msvcrt.html

**B-measurement**

- Cost of the Windows bulk snapshot (NtQuerySystemInformation) in Python, and of the whole tick on Windows. Why: This machine is Linux; vendor documentation gives no timings How to check: On the Windows 11 machine, port docs/review/probes/measure/snapshot_cost.py to a ctypes SystemProcessInformation parser and run it with about 300 processes, while the machine is quiet
- Whether QueryPerformanceCounter advances during sleep. Why: Needs a Windows machine; the documentation fetched does not say How to check: See would_confirm on the suspend finding
- Windows CPU time resolution per process, and the semantics of the System\Processor Queue Length and Processor Information frequency counters used by the verdict. Why: Not settled by documentation within this run's budget; needs Windows How to check: On Windows, sample GetProcessTimes for a busy-loop process every 100 ms and look at the step size; read the counter descriptions in perfmon or with typeperf -q
- Nested Job Object accounting (whether a parent job's totals include child jobs). Why: The fetch of learn.microsoft.com nested-jobs returned page metadata only in the earlier attempt; not retried How to check: Read https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/procthread/nested-jobs, section on accounting, and test with a job inside a job
- `why` latency under 2 s and the P4 30-day range load under 1 s. Why: The instructions forbid new timing runs while other reviewers are active, and there is no implementation to time How to check: Time `submeter why` against a raw file holding 5 minutes of 300-process data on the target machine once built
- Server-side CPU and memory against the 150 MB and 1%/3% budget. Why: No server exists in the baseline How to check: Measure collector plus server private bytes during the P1 soak
- Process counts on GitHub-hosted Windows and Linux runners, compared with the 300 processes the budget assumes. Why: Needs runner access How to check: In CI: python -c "import psutil;print(len(psutil.pids()))" on both runners

**C-storage**

- Whether Windows refuses deleting a raw file while a read-only SQLite connection or only the -shm mapping is open, and how anti-virus scanning affects it. Why: This machine is Linux; the finding rests on SQLite source and Microsoft documentation only How to check: On Windows: uv run python scripts/platform_check.py and read the 'raw file on disk' line; or open the file with sqlite3.connect('file:PATH?mode=ro', uri=True) in one process and call os.remove(PATH) from another
- Actual data loss on power loss or OS crash with synchronous=NORMAL. Why: Cannot cut power here; the claim rests on sqlite.org and on strace sync counts How to check: On a VM: run the collector, hard-reset the VM, then compare MAX(ts) in workload_raw with the last tick the collector logged
- How often Windows steps the wall clock backwards (time service, resume from hibernate, dual boot with the hardware clock in local time). Why: Needs a Windows machine and vendor documentation not consulted in this attempt How to check: On Windows: log time.time() minus time.monotonic() every second for a day, including a sleep and a resume, and look for negative steps
- Whether time.monotonic counts suspended time on Windows. Why: Needs a real suspend on Windows; either answer is a gap by one of the sampler's two rules How to check: docs/reference/windows-priorities.md section 5: run submeter top --seconds 60 across a one-minute sleep
- Timing of any storage path (checkpoint cost, synchronous=FULL cost). Why: The task forbids re-timing while other reviewers run; only sync counts were taken How to check: In phase 1 on the target laptop: time 1000 ticks under NORMAL plus a 5 s checkpoint against FULL
- Hot and cold set handling in the Sampler (long_intervals.py part 2: hot-only snapshots counted 2400 starts and 2400 exits and kept 300 of 372 CPU-s; complete snapshots with cold counters refreshed every 10 s gave p95 1.73 cores against a true 0.62). Why: Measured, but it belongs to the measurement lens (b-measurement/hot_cold_sampler); not reported as a separate finding here How to check: docs/review/probes/c-storage/long_intervals.out.txt part 2 and b-measurement/hot_cold_sampler.out.txt

**D-privacy**

- Windows file ACLs for folders made under %LOCALAPPDATA% with the default mode and with os.mkdir(0o700), and what a SUBMETER_HOME on a drive root inherits. Why: Linux machine; the Microsoft documentation says only that ACLs are inherited from the parent folder How to check: On Windows, not elevated: uv run python docs/review/probes/d-privacy/win_confirm.py (check 1 prints icacls output)
- Whether CreateProcess with CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB fails inside a job that sets no limits (F14). Why: Needs Windows; the documentation states the requirement but not the failure How to check: win_confirm.py check 2
- Windows loopback port sharing (SO_REUSEADDR and SO_EXCLUSIVEADDRUSE) and the order in which localhost resolves (::1 first?). Why: Needs Windows; the earlier fetch of Microsoft's SO_REUSEADDR page came back truncated How to check: win_confirm.py checks 3 and 4
- Whether a standard Windows user can register a logon scheduled task (F13). Why: Needs Windows; the vendor sentence is ambiguous in scope How to check: The schtasks and Register-ScheduledTask commands in F13's would_confirm
- Whether psutil on Windows ever returns an unsplit argv, or lone UTF-16 surrogates, in cmdline() (F2, F3). Why: psutil splits in C (cext.proc_cmdline), which is not readable here How to check: On Windows: python -c "import psutil; print(sum(1 for p in psutil.process_iter(['cmdline']) if p.info['cmdline'] and len(p.info['cmdline'])==1 and ' ' in p.info['cmdline'][0]))"
- Running FastAPI, uvicorn or Starlette to observe /docs CDN loads, access-log lines and WebSocket header handling. Why: None of them is installed in .venv, and installing is forbidden; behaviour taken from vendor docs and uvicorn source on GitHub How to check: In a scratch environment with fastapi and uvicorn: start a default app, open /docs with the browser's network panel, and send GET /x?token=abc to see the access log line
- Whether hosted agents send MCP tool results to their model provider (F10's 'what leaves the machine' point). Why: This is reasoning about agent architecture, not a vendor statement I fetched How to check: Cite the agent vendor's data-handling documentation for tool results
- Final Rule 3 check of desktop notifications on Windows (toast) and Linux (D-Bus). Why: Notification code is not built and the brief names no library How to check: When phase 4 picks a library, check its documentation for any network use

**E-baseline**

- Every Windows behaviour: pid 0 idle time, busy-sum double count, lock release timing after a crash, private bytes from psutil on real protected processes. Why: This machine is Linux; nothing may be installed or run on Windows from here How to check: On Windows 11 run the would_confirm commands in the pid 0, busy-sum and lock findings, then uv run python scripts/platform_check.py
- The 20 tests under Python 3.12. Why: The .venv is 3.14 with compiled wheels; a 3.12 environment would need packages installed How to check: uv run --python 3.12 pytest (in a throwaway copy)
- How often psutil returns an empty /proc/PID/io or a zombie for short-lived children on a spawn-heavy load. Why: Needs a sustained spawning load and timing; timing probes were excluded to avoid disturbing other reviewers How to check: Run PsutilProvider.snapshot() in a loop beside the reconcile_under_spawn spawner and count RuntimeError, ValueError and ZombieProcess per 1000 snapshots
- Plateau test on a staircase leak (lead 10). Why: Not reached within this attempt How to check: Feed check_growth a series that rises 100 MB then holds for 40 minutes, repeatedly, over 6 hours, and see whether plateaued() clears it
- Redaction gaps in privacy/redact.py. Why: Outside the priority list for this lens; the lead reviewer's lead 5 covers it How to check: redact_cmdline on 'curl -H "Authorization: Bearer x"', '-u user:pass', '--env=TOKEN=x', '/pass:x' and a URL query token
- Cost of reading PSS for every readable process (would decide the memory-total question). Why: A timing measurement; excluded while other reviewers run How to check: Time memory_full_info() over all own processes on a quiet machine, as probes/measure/snapshot_cost.py does

**H-scope**

- Whether answering 34 min to 33 h windows from raw merged at query time (after cutting the minute tier) stays fast enough for `why` and charts. Why: The task forbids new timing while other reviewers run How to check: On a quiet machine, build a synthetic 48 h raw file at 2 s with 20 workloads and time a 24 h query merged into 2000 steps, median of 5
- Bulk-snapshot cost and the 2 s default on Windows at the owner's process count. Why: This machine is Linux; all timings are Linux How to check: P0 probe on the owner's machine as a standard user: a NtQuerySystemInformation(SystemProcessInformation) prototype through ctypes, 20 runs, with the process count, plus the per-tick Python work over the same rows
- Which OS the build sessions will run on, and whether the owner uses Linux at all (decides the one-OS finding). Why: prompts/01:3 says Windows while this review runs on Linux; it is a fact only the owner knows How to check: Ask the owner

Why: Windows only; leads D and G mark it suspected How to check: On the owner's machine, unelevated: schtasks /Create /SC ONLOGON /TN SubmeterProbe /TR "cmd /c exit 0" /RU %USERNAME% /IT, then schtasks /Delete /TN SubmeterProbe /F; record the exit codes

**Gaps the coverage check found in the lenses**

- A, docs/09:15 and docs/10:53: the Windows claim that temperatures have no dependable source without administrator rights is not addressed by any finding, nothing_found line or not_checked item. The 'temperatur' search hits only A-linux, H-scope and R150. R150 treats temperatures only as a scope question (defer). Throttling detected from frequency is addressed by the A-windows not_checked item on Processor Information counters.
- A, docs/09:11-12 (the Windows 'Own user only' rows for memory and disk I/O): the A-windows nothing_found line says psutil's memory_info (private) and io_counters fall back to the whole-system query for processes it cannot open. That means private bytes and I/O of other users' processes are readable on Windows, but nobody says the docs/09 rows are therefore wrong for Windows. docs/09:12 itself notes that Windows process I/O counters include non-file I/O, and GetProcessIoCounters covers 'all I/O operations performed by the specified process'. Nobody follows that through to the 'Disk bytes read and written' catalogue metric (docs/06) or the I/O verdict. Windows USS (how it is read, its cost, for which processes) is not addressed either.
- A, the named area 'what performance counters return without elevation' (docs/09:17, :20): not_checked names only GPU Engine, GPU Process Memory, Processor Information and Processor Queue Length. Two other Windows readings are in no item: the counters the memory and I/O verdicts need (commit, page writes, PhysicalDisk queue and latency), and the battery reading behind on_battery_multiplier.
- A, the named area 'what security software or policy on a work computer blocks': Defender ASR is covered (R129 and a not_checked item), but application-control policy is in no item. That means AppLocker, App Control for Business (WDAC) and Smart App Control. On managed machines these commonly block executables in user-writable folders, which is where uv-managed Python (%APPDATA%\uv\python), .venv\Scripts\python.exe and the submeter.exe console script live. So policy decides whether the collector or the logon task can run at all. How to check: Get-AppLockerPolicy -Effective -Xml; Get-CimInstance -Namespace root\Microsoft\Windows\DeviceGuard -ClassName Win32_DeviceGuard (CodeIntegrityPolicyEnforcementStatus, UsermodeCodeIntegrityPolicyEnforcementStatus); then run .venv\Scripts\submeter.exe doctor as a standard user.
- A, windows-priorities.md item 1 (the baseline checks run on Windows): no not_checked item lists the five checks on Windows or the item's named risks, which are a test's temporary folder that cannot be removed while a database file is open, and path comparisons that assume forward slashes. Only R79 names one Windows test failure (the locked byte read through a second handle). E-baseline's Windows item points only to platform_check.py, and the pytest items in not_checked concern Python 3.12/3.13, not Windows.
- A, windows-priorities.md item 8 (a swarm on Windows): three points are in no item: line endings under the LF pin in .gitattributes, a virtual environment that a running process holds open, and the absence of sandboxing on native Windows. G's nothing_found covers only path length and parallel pytest temp folders. These matter mainly at stage 3, but lens A was told to work through the file in its order.
- G, the named area 'phases that are too large': no finding or nothing_found line rules on phase size. G's measurements count components per phase (P0 3, P1 9, P2 4, P3 3, P4 3, P5 1, P6 2, P7 0), and R143's impact says 'P1 is the largest phase'. No item says whether P1 fits one gate or should be split. R144 and R147 shrink it only indirectly.
- H, the execution prompt lists 'the baseline code' as open to challenge: no H finding or nothing_found line says whether the build should extend the baseline (docs/14:78) or replace parts of it, given R114 (the tests catch 1 of 29 deliberately broken behaviours) and R116 (the models cannot express most of the catalogue). R116's single restructuring task is the nearest item. The other open choices the prompt names are addressed: equal weight of the jobs (R148), service, dashboard and CLI (R152 and the H nothing_found line), extra telemetry (R150), Python (H nothing_found), phase order (R143, R155), and the 25 components (R155).
- Not checked contradicts R68 and R138: the A-windows item 'Monotonic clock across suspend on Python 3.13+' and the C-storage item 'Whether time.monotonic counts suspended time on Windows' both tell the owner to run `submeter top --seconds 60` across a one-minute sleep. R68 and R138 show that this check cannot fail, because any interval over 30 s is a gap and prints zero CPU. These items should use an interval under 30 s across the sleep and read the gap flag, as R138 proposes.
